<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Milne's rationality conjecture for abelian varieties, including residue characteristic 2. After good reduction, the pairing of a rational Hodge class with any complementary product of divisor classes on the reduction is the same rational number in every prime-to-<i>p</i> realization and in crystalline cohomology. Using the Hodge theorem for CM abelian varieties, we also show that every such specialized Hodge class is represented by a single rational algebraic cycle in all these realizations.

- **claim-main-rationality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of dimension $d$ with good reduction $A_0/\mathbb F$ at $w$. Let $0\leq r\leq d$, and let $$\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$$ be a rational Hodge class. For every list of Cartier divisors $D_1,\ldots,D_{d-r}$ on $A_0$, there is a number $q\in\mathbb Q$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_\ell\bigl(\gamma_{0,\ell} c_{1,\ell}(D_1)\cdots c_{1,\ell}(D_{d-r})\bigr)&=q &&\text{in }\mathbb Q_\ell\quad(\ell\ne p),\\ \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_p\bigl(\gamma_{0,p} c_{1,\mathrm{cris}}(D_1)\cdots c_{1,\mathrm{cris}}(D_{d-r})\bigr)&=q &&\text{in }C_w. \end{aligned}$$ Here $\gamma_{0,\ell}$ is the smooth proper specialization of the $\ell$-adic realization, and $\gamma_{0,p}$ is the de Rham–crystalline specialization in $$H^{2r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p](r) \otimes_{K_0}C_w.$$ The traces use the top Tate twist and send the class of a zero-cycle to its degree. If $r=d$, the divisor product is $1$.
  - Explanation: For an abelian variety with good reduction at the chosen place, a rational Hodge class paired with any complementary product of divisors on the reduction gives one rational number. That number is the same in every prime-to-p realization and in crystalline cohomology. This is Milne's rationality conjecture.
- **claim-comparison-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison)): For an abelian variety with good reduction, the realizations of an absolute Hodge class are compatible with the de Rham and crystalline comparison isomorphisms. In particular, the $p$-adic crystalline tensor obtained from its $p$-adic étale realization agrees, after extension of scalars, with the de Rham–crystalline realization in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. These compatibilities respect tensor operations, duals, Tate twists, divisor classes, and rational homomorphisms.* *The same assertion applies to auxiliary abelian varieties with good reduction over a finite extension of $K_0$, using a complex embedding of an algebraic closure of their field of definition.
  - Explanation: With good reduction, the realizations of an absolute Hodge class agree under the de Rham and crystalline comparison isomorphisms. These compatibilities respect tensor operations, duals, twists, divisor classes and rational homomorphisms.
- **claim-pairing-pullback-closed** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pairing-functorial)): If $\alpha$ has the Lefschetz pairing property on $Y$ and $f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^0(X,Y)$, then $f^*\alpha$ has that property on $X$.
  - Explanation: The Lefschetz pairing property is preserved when a class is pulled back along a rational homomorphism of abelian varieties.
- **claim-lift-rational-hom** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lift-hom)): Let $\mathcal X,\mathcal Y$ be abelian schemes over $R$, with special fibers $X_0,Y_0$, and let $f_0\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^0(X_0,Y_0)$. If its contravariant crystalline map preserves the Hodge filtrations, then $f_0$ lifts uniquely to a rational homomorphism $\mathcal X\to\mathcal Y$.* *In particular, a symmetric rational homomorphism $X_0\to X_0^\vee$ with this property lifts to a rational divisor class on the generic fiber. Algebraic identities among finitely many such lifted maps remain valid.
  - Explanation: A rational homomorphism of special fibers whose crystalline map preserves the Hodge filtrations lifts uniquely to a rational homomorphism of the abelian schemes. Symmetric ones give rational divisor classes on the generic fiber.
- **claim-transport-quasi-isogeny** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-transport)): Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of positive dimension with good reduction at $w$, and put $V=H_1(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q)$. Suppose $$G\subset\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\psi)$$ is connected reductive, its Hodge homomorphism $h$ gives the Hodge structure of $A$, and its conjugacy class $X$ makes this inclusion a Hodge embedding. Let $\gamma$ be a rational Hodge class of codimension $r$, represented on $V$ by an alternating form satisfying <a href="#eq:transport-multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-multiplier">[eq:transport-multiplier]</a>.* *Then there exist a positive integer $n$, an auxiliary good-reduction abelian variety $B$, a quasi-isogeny $a:A_0^n\to B_0$, and a rational Hodge class $\kappa$ of codimension $r$ on $B$ such that, for the first-factor inclusion $j:A_0\to A_0^n$, $$(aj)^*\kappa_0=\gamma_0. \label{eq:transport-class}$$ The equality holds simultaneously in every $\ell$-adic realization with $\ell\ne p$ and in the crystalline realization after extension to $C_w$. Either of the following two further specifications can be imposed.* 1. *The variety $B$ is of CM type and is defined over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, with its indicated good reduction at $w$.* 2. *Put $D_A=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p]$. Suppose $\mathcal F_A$ is a filtration in degrees $0,1$ on $D_A$, over a finite extension of $K_0$, which is weakly admissible and has finite local-field descent together with the specialized tensors specifying the $G$-structure. Suppose also that, after a geometric tensor-preserving identification, it lies in the $G$-orbit of the original Hodge filtration. Then $B$ can be defined over a finite extension $K/K_0$ inside $C_w$, and $a$ can be chosen so that $$a^*\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(B/K) =(\mathcal F_A^1)^{\oplus n}. \label{eq:transport-prescribed-filtration}$$ Here the equality is made after a common finite extension and crystalline–de Rham identification. To discuss Hodge classes on this $B$, choose a complex embedding of an algebraic closure of $K$ extending the fixed embedding of $\overline{\mathbb Q}$.* *In the second case the realizations of $\kappa$ are supplied by the canonical tensor sections of the Hodge-type model and the polarization. In the first case these agree with the usual realizations and specialization of its absolute Hodge class.
  - Explanation: A rational Hodge class on an abelian variety is transported, by one quasi-isogeny on special fibers from a power, to a Hodge class on an auxiliary good-reduction variety, with the same equality in every realization. The auxiliary variety can be taken either of CM type or realizing a prescribed admissible filtration.
- **claim-auxiliary-hodge-datum** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-auxiliary-datum)): Let $$(G,X)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow (\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\psi),X_{\mathrm{Siegel}})$$ be a Hodge embedding, with $G$ connected and similitude character $c$. For every prime $p$ there exist a totally real field $P$, a connected reductive group $J$, and a Hodge embedding $$(J,X_J)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow (\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(W,\psi_W),X'_{\mathrm{Siegel}})$$ with the following properties.* 1. *There are inclusions of Shimura data induced by $$G\longrightarrow J\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P/\mathbb Q}G_P,$$ where the first map is diagonal and $J$ is the subgroup on which the similitude multipliers in the restriction of scalars agree.* 2. *The group $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$ is quasi-split and has a full connected stabilizer $\mathcal J$ making $(J,X_J,\mathcal J)$ strongly admissible.* 3. *As a rational $J$-representation, $$W\simeq (V\otimes_{\mathbb Q}P)^{\oplus m} \label{eq:transport-copies}$$ for some positive integer $m$. The form $\psi_W$ has multiplier the common character $c$, and $\mathcal J\to\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(W_{\mathbb Q_p})$ extends to a closed immersion into $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(\Lambda_p)$ for a self-dual $\mathbb Z_p$-lattice $\Lambda_p$.
  - Explanation: For every prime, the given Hodge datum embeds into an enlarged datum built from a totally real field. The local group is quasi-split with a strongly admissible integral structure, and the representation is a sum of copies of the original one.
- **claim-realize-admissible-filtration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-admissible-realization)): Let $(J,X_J,\mathcal J)$ be strongly admissible, with $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$ quasi-split, and choose the adapted embedding and neat level above. Let $x\in\mathcal S_K(\mathbb F)$ have a lift to characteristic zero. Suppose a filtration $\mathcal F$ on $D_x$, after a finite extension of $K_0$, has the following properties:* 1. *the resulting filtered isocrystal is admissible;* 2. *geometrically its filtration is induced by a $J$-valued cocharacter in the conjugacy class prescribed by $X_J$.* *Then, after extending that field finitely, there are $y\in\mathcal I_x$, a tensor-preserving quasi-isogeny $b:C_x\to C_y$, and a lift $\widetilde y$ over the extended valuation ring such that $$b^*\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1 H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(C_{\widetilde y})=\mathcal F^1$$ under the crystalline–de Rham identification.* *In particular, condition <span class="upright">(1)</span> follows if the filtered isocrystal and its tensor data have finite local-field descent and $\mathcal F$ is weakly admissible.
  - Explanation: An admissible filtration of the prescribed group type on the crystalline module is realized, after a finite extension, by a lift of an abelian variety in the tensor isogeny class, through a tensor-preserving quasi-isogeny.
- **claim-cm-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cm-transport)): Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of positive dimension with good reduction at $w$, and let $\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$ be a rational Hodge class, where $0\leq r\leq\dim A$. There are a positive integer $n$, a CM abelian variety $B/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$, a rational Hodge class $\kappa$ of codimension $r$ on $B$, and a quasi-isogeny $a:A_0^n\to B_0$ such that $$\gamma_0=(aj)^*\kappa_0,$$ where $j:A_0\to A_0^n$ is the first-factor inclusion. This is an equality of the usual absolute-Hodge specializations in every prime-to-$p$ realization and in crystalline cohomology extended to $C_w$.
  - Explanation: Every rational Hodge class on a good-reduction abelian variety is the pullback, along one quasi-isogeny on special fibers, of a rational Hodge class on a CM abelian variety, in all prime-to-p realizations and crystalline cohomology.
- **claim-filtration-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-filtration)): Let $A_0/\overline{\mathbb F}_p$ be an abelian variety equipped with a rational action of a CM field $E$ and a polarization whose Rosati involution restricts to conjugation on $E$. Suppose that $$D=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p))[1/p]$$ has rank $2s$ over $E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0$, where $s\geq 1$. Suppose also that there is an invertible $u\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A_0)$ such that $$u^\dagger=u,\qquad ue=\bar e u\quad(e\in E).$$ Let $\beta$ be the dual polarization pairing on $D$, with values in the isocrystal line of slope $1$.* *Then there are a finite extension $K/K_0$ and a subspace $L\subset D_K$ with the following properties:* 1. *The one-step filtration with $\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1D_K=L$ is weakly admissible.* 2. *$L$ is stable under the cohomological actions of $E$ and $u$, and is Lagrangian for $\beta$.* 3. *After extending to an algebraically closed field $\Omega/K$, one has $\dim_\Omega L_\tau=s$ for every embedding $\tau:E\hookrightarrow\Omega$.* 4. *The isocrystal, its indicated tensors, and this filtration have finite local field descent.* *Moreover, geometrically $L$ belongs to the balanced polarized cocharacter orbit. More explicitly, define $$G_D=\left\{g\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(D,\beta)\cap \mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_{E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0}(D): \det_{E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0}(g)=c(g)^s\right\},$$ where the scalar on the right is viewed in $(E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0)^\times$. The balanced $E$-stable $\beta$-Lagrangians form a single $G_D(\Omega)$-orbit. Each is the weight-$1$ space of a cocharacter of $G_{D,\Omega}$ with weights $0,1$. In particular $L$ belongs to the orbit of any original filtration of this type under a tensor-preserving comparison.
  - Explanation: On the crystalline isocrystal of an abelian variety with CM action and an invertible symmetric endomorphism skew-commuting with the CM field, there is a weakly admissible one-step filtration over a finite extension. It is stable under the actions, Lagrangian for the polarization, balanced at every embedding, and has finite local field descent.
- **claim-bounded-degree-avoidance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-avoidance)): Let $K_1/K_0$ be a finite extension, and let $r,b\geq 1$ be integers. There exist an element $\theta$ algebraic over $\mathbb Q_p$ and a point $$(x_0,\ldots,x_{r-1})\in K_1(\theta)^r$$ at which every nonzero polynomial in $K_1[X_0,\ldots,X_{r-1}]$ of total degree at most $b$ is nonzero. The coordinates $x_i$ can all be chosen in $\mathbb Q_p(\theta)$.
  - Explanation: One point over a finite extension of the coefficient field avoids the zero loci of all nonzero polynomials of bounded total degree over the fixed field.
- **claim-lagrangian-minimal-intersection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection)): Let $M$ be a symplectic space of dimension $2m$ over a field $k$ of characteristic zero, and let $W\subset M$ have dimension $a$. A nonempty open subset of the Lagrangian Grassmannian consists of subspaces $L$ such that $$\dim(L\cap W)=\max(0,a-m).$$ On any fixed symplectic affine big cell, at least one nonzero polynomial of degree at most $m$ has nonvanishing locus contained in this open subset. This degree bound is independent of $W$.
  - Explanation: A generic Lagrangian meets a fixed subspace in the smallest possible dimension, and this is enforced by a nonzero polynomial whose degree bound does not depend on the subspace.
- **claim-filtration-admissible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-filtration-admissible)): The filtration of Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a>, with its finite local field descent, is admissible: the descended filtered isocrystal is the filtered crystalline module of a crystalline representation.
  - Explanation: The weakly admissible filtration with its finite local field descent is in fact admissible, being the filtered crystalline module of a crystalline representation.
- **claim-filtration-divisor-condition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-filtration-divisor)): Let $D,L$ be as in Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a>, and let $\psi$ and $U$ denote the homological polarization and the homological action of $u$ on $D_K^\vee$. The untwisted divisor form $\psi(\cdot,U\cdot)$ belongs to $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1\bigl(\bigwedge^2D_K\bigr)=L\wedge D_K,$$ and hence to filtration degree zero after its codimension-one Tate twist. If a marked power is realized by an abelian lift, the corresponding pulled-back divisor satisfies the filtration condition for its symmetric homomorphism in Lemma <a href="#lem:lift-hom" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lift-hom">5</a>.
  - Explanation: The untwisted divisor form attached to the invertible endomorphism lies in the first filtration step of the second exterior power, so the divisor satisfies the filtration condition needed to lift its symmetric homomorphism.
- **claim-weil-dichotomy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-weil)): Every $\gamma\in W_E^s(A)$ satisfies the Lefschetz pairing property at $w$. More precisely, if $\mathcal U$ in <a href="#eq:weil-U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-U">[eq:weil-U]</a> has no invertible element, all complementary pairings are zero. If it has an invertible element, $\gamma_0$ is a simultaneous rational Lefschetz class.
  - Explanation: Weil classes satisfy the Lefschetz pairing property at the place. If the endomorphism space has no invertible element all complementary pairings vanish; otherwise the specialized class is a simultaneous rational Lefschetz class.
- **claim-weil-orthogonality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weil-orthogonality)): If $\mathcal U$ contains no invertible element, every specialization of a class in $W_E^s(A)$ is orthogonal to all complementary Lefschetz classes, in each cohomological realization.
  - Explanation: When the endomorphism space contains no invertible element, specialized Weil classes are orthogonal to all complementary Lefschetz classes in every realization.
- **claim-weil-divisor-span** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span)): Let $B$ be a complex abelian variety, let $R\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(B)$ be a rational projector, and suppose that there is an algebra action $E\longrightarrow R\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(B)R$ whose unit maps to $R$. Let $T=\operatorname{im}(R)$ in rational first homology, with $\dim_E T=2s$; the indicated endomorphisms act by zero on $\ker(R)$. Suppose there is a rational divisor class $D$ on $B$ such that $R^*D=D$ and, after splitting $E$, its alternating form is $$D=\sum_\tau D_\tau, \qquad D_\tau\in\bigwedge\nolimits^2 T_\tau^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q(1),$$ with each $D_\tau$ nondegenerate. Then the rational subspace whose scalar extension is $$\bigoplus_\tau\bigwedge\nolimits^{2s}T_\tau^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q(s),$$ extended by zero on the complementary summand, consists of rational Lefschetz classes on $B$.
  - Explanation: A divisor that is nondegenerate on each eigenspace of a rational summand has powers that generate the determinant classes of that summand, so these are rational Lefschetz classes.
- **claim-weil-group-datum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weil-group)): The Hodge homomorphism of $A$ factors through $G$, and its conjugacy class defines a Shimura datum of Hodge type through the displayed embedding. Over an algebraically closed coefficient field, the orbit of the corresponding filtration on $H^1$ consists exactly of the $E$-stable Lagrangians for the dual polarization pairing whose dimension at each embedding of $E$ is $s$. Using a basis of its Tate line, every alternating form in <a href="#eq:weil-pure-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-pure-space">[eq:weil-pure-space]</a> transforms by $c^s$.
  - Explanation: The Hodge homomorphism factors through the determinant-constrained similitude group, which gives a Hodge-type Shimura datum. Its filtration orbit is the balanced Lagrangians, and the Weil forms scale by a power of the multiplier.
- **claim-cm-reduction-weil** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cm-reduction)): Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of CM type with good reduction at $w$, and let $r>0$. For every rational Hodge class $\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$, there are finitely many abelian varieties $B_i/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$, rational homomorphisms $f_i\colon A\to B_i$, and rational Weil classes $\eta_i\in H^{2r}(B_i(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$ such that $$\label{eq:cm-pullback-decomposition} \gamma=\sum_i f_i^*\eta_i.$$ Each $B_i$ has an action of a CM field $K$ for which $H_1(B_i(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q)$ has rank $2r$ over $K$, the Hodge signatures are $(r,r)$ at every embedding of $K$, and a polarization induces complex conjugation on $K$. The equality specializes simultaneously in all the realizations used in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: On a CM abelian variety with good reduction, every positive-codimension rational Hodge class is a finite sum of pullbacks of rational Weil classes from good-reduction varieties with balanced signatures, compatibly in all realizations.
- **claim-cm-pairing-property** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cm-pairing)): The Lefschetz pairing property holds for the specialization of every rational Hodge class on a CM abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$.
  - Explanation: The Lefschetz pairing property holds for specializations of rational Hodge classes on CM abelian varieties, by combining the Weil reduction, the Weil dichotomy and pullback closure.
- **claim-all-hodge-fixed-theory** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-all-hodge-specialization)): For every abelian variety $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ of dimension $d$ with good reduction $A_0/\mathbb F$ at $w$, every $0\leq r\leq d$, and every rational Hodge class $$\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r)),$$ there is a unique class $\rho_\gamma\in\mathcal R^r(A_0)$ such that $$(\rho_\gamma)_\ell=\gamma_{0,\ell}\quad(\ell\ne p),\qquad (\rho_\gamma)_p\otimes 1=\gamma_{0,p}.$$ The prime-to-$p$ equalities are in $H^{2r}_{\acute{e}t}(A_0,\mathbb Q_\ell(r))$; the crystalline equality is in $$H^{2r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p](r) \otimes_{K_0}C_w.$$ The specialization components are those of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. The assertion holds for every prime $p$, including $p=2$, with the prime, place, and theory fixed before $A$ and $\gamma$ vary.
  - Explanation: Every rational Hodge class on a good-reduction abelian variety specializes to a unique class in one fixed good theory of rational Tate classes, with the prescribed components in all realizations, including residue characteristic 2.
- **claim-algebraic-specialization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-algebraic-specialization)): Fix the prime and place as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of dimension $d$ with good reduction $A_0/\mathbb F$ at $w$, and let $\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$ be a rational Hodge class, where $0\leq r\leq d$. There is one rational algebraic cycle $z_\gamma\in\operatorname{CH}^r(A_0)_{\mathbb Q}$ such that $$\operatorname{cl}_\ell(z_\gamma)=\gamma_{0,\ell} \quad(\ell\ne p),\qquad \operatorname{cl}_{\mathrm{cris}}(z_\gamma)\otimes1=\gamma_{0,p}.$$ The crystalline equality is in $$H^{2r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p](r) \otimes_{K_0}C_w.$$ This holds for every prime $p$, including $p=2$. Consequently, for every $y\in\operatorname{CH}^{d-r}(A_0)_{\mathbb Q}$, the traces of $\gamma_{0,\ell}\operatorname{cl}_\ell(y)$ for $\ell\ne p$ and of $\gamma_{0,p}\operatorname{cl}_{\mathrm{cris}}(y)$ are all the same rational number $\deg(z_\gamma\cdot y)$.
  - Explanation: Using the Hodge theorem for CM abelian varieties as an extra input, each specialized Hodge class is represented by one rational algebraic cycle on the reduction in all realizations. Pairings with complementary algebraic cycles are then the rational intersection number.

        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of positive dimension with good reduction at $w$, and put $V=H_1(A(\\mathbb C),\\mathbb Q)$. Suppose $$G\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\\psi)$$ is connected reductive, its Hodge homomorphism $h$ gives the Hodge structure of $A$, and its conjugacy class $X$ makes this inclusion a Hodge embedding. Let $\\gamma$ be a rational Hodge class of codimension $r$, represented on $V$ by an alternating form satisfying <a href=\"#eq:transport-multiplier\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:transport-multiplier\">[eq:transport-multiplier]</a>.*\n\n\n*Then there exist a positive integer $n$, an auxiliary good-reduction abelian variety $B$, a quasi-isogeny $a:A_0^n\\to B_0$, and a rational Hodge class $\\kappa$ of codimension $r$ on $B$ such that, for the first-factor inclusion $j:A_0\\to A_0^n$, $$(aj)^*\\kappa_0=\\gamma_0.\n \\label{eq:transport-class}$$ The equality holds simultaneously in every $\\ell$-adic realization with $\\ell\\ne p$ and in the crystalline realization after extension to $C_w$. Either of the following two further specifications can be imposed.*\n\n1.  *The variety $B$ is of CM type and is defined over $\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$, with its indicated good reduction at $w$.*\n\n\n2.  *Put $D_A=H^1_{\\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\\mathbb F))[1/p]$. Suppose $\\mathcal F_A$ is a filtration in degrees $0,1$ on $D_A$, over a finite extension of $K_0$, which is weakly admissible and has finite local-field descent together with the specialized tensors specifying the $G$-structure. Suppose also that, after a geometric tensor-preserving identification, it lies in the $G$-orbit of the original Hodge filtration. Then $B$ can be defined over a finite extension $K/K_0$ inside $C_w$, and $a$ can be chosen so that $$a^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fil}}^1H^1_{\\mathrm{dR}}(B/K)\n           =(\\mathcal F_A^1)^{\\oplus n}.\n     \\label{eq:transport-prescribed-filtration}$$ Here the equality is made after a common finite extension and crystalline–de Rham identification. To discuss Hodge classes on this $B$, choose a complex embedding of an algebraic closure of $K$ extending the fixed embedding of $\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$.*\n\n*In the second case the realizations of $\\kappa$ are supplied by the canonical tensor sections of the Hodge-type model and the polarization. In the first case these agree with the usual realizations and specialization of its absolute Hodge class.",
      "explanation": "A rational Hodge class on an abelian variety is transported, by one quasi-isogeny on special fibers from a power, to a Hodge class on an auxiliary good-reduction variety, with the same equality in every realization. The auxiliary variety can be taken either of CM type or realizing a prescribed admissible filtration.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-auxiliary-datum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$(G,X)\\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow\n (\\mathop{\\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\\psi),X_{\\mathrm{Siegel}})$$ be a Hodge embedding, with $G$ connected and similitude character $c$. For every prime $p$ there exist a totally real field $P$, a connected reductive group $J$, and a Hodge embedding $$(J,X_J)\\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow\n (\\mathop{\\mathrm{GSp}}(W,\\psi_W),X'_{\\mathrm{Siegel}})$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *There are inclusions of Shimura data induced by $$G\\longrightarrow J\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{Res}}_{P/\\mathbb Q}G_P,$$ where the first map is diagonal and $J$ is the subgroup on which the similitude multipliers in the restriction of scalars agree.*\n\n2.  *The group $J_{\\mathbb Q_p}$ is quasi-split and has a full connected stabilizer $\\mathcal J$ making $(J,X_J,\\mathcal J)$ strongly admissible.*\n\n\n3.  *As a rational $J$-representation, $$W\\simeq (V\\otimes_{\\mathbb Q}P)^{\\oplus m}\n     \\label{eq:transport-copies}$$ for some positive integer $m$. The form $\\psi_W$ has multiplier the common character $c$, and $\\mathcal J\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}(W_{\\mathbb Q_p})$ extends to a closed immersion into $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}(\\Lambda_p)$ for a self-dual $\\mathbb Z_p$-lattice $\\Lambda_p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-admissible-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(J,X_J,\\mathcal J)$ be strongly admissible, with $J_{\\mathbb Q_p}$ quasi-split, and choose the adapted embedding and neat level above. Let $x\\in\\mathcal S_K(\\mathbb F)$ have a lift to characteristic zero. Suppose a filtration $\\mathcal F$ on $D_x$, after a finite extension of $K_0$, has the following properties:*\n\n1.  *the resulting filtered isocrystal is admissible;*\n\n2.  *geometrically its filtration is induced by a $J$-valued cocharacter in the conjugacy class prescribed by $X_J$.*\n\n*Then, after extending that field finitely, there are $y\\in\\mathcal I_x$, a tensor-preserving quasi-isogeny $b:C_x\\to C_y$, and a lift $\\widetilde y$ over the extended valuation ring such that $$b^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fil}}^1 H^1_{\\mathrm{dR}}(C_{\\widetilde y})=\\mathcal F^1$$ under the crystalline–de Rham identification.*\n\n*In particular, condition <span class=\"upright\">(1)</span> follows if the filtered isocrystal and its tensor data have finite local-field descent and $\\mathcal F$ is weakly admissible.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-cm-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of positive dimension with good reduction at $w$, and let $\\gamma\\in H^{2r}(A(\\mathbb C),\\mathbb Q(r))$ be a rational Hodge class, where $0\\leq r\\leq\\dim A$. There are a positive integer $n$, a CM abelian variety $B/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$, a rational Hodge class $\\kappa$ of codimension $r$ on $B$, and a quasi-isogeny $a:A_0^n\\to B_0$ such that $$\\gamma_0=(aj)^*\\kappa_0,$$ where $j:A_0\\to A_0^n$ is the first-factor inclusion. This is an equality of the usual absolute-Hodge specializations in every prime-to-$p$ realization and in crystalline cohomology extended to $C_w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-cm-transport",

          "source_id": "PM-001.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $A/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of positive dimension with good reduction at $w$, and let $\\gamma\\in H^{2r}(A(\\mathbb C),\\mathbb Q(r))$ be a rational Hodge class, where $0\\leq r\\leq\\dim A$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-filtration",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A_0/\\overline{\\mathbb F}_p$ be an abelian variety equipped with a rational action of a CM field $E$ and a polarization whose Rosati involution restricts to conjugation on $E$. Suppose that $$D=H^1_{\\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\\overline{\\mathbb F}_p))[1/p]$$ has rank $2s$ over $E\\otimes_\\mathbb QK_0$, where $s\\geq 1$. Suppose also that there is an invertible $u\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}^0(A_0)$ such that $$u^\\dagger=u,\\qquad ue=\\bar e u\\quad(e\\in E).$$ Let $\\beta$ be the dual polarization pairing on $D$, with values in the isocrystal line of slope $1$.*\n\n*Then there are a finite extension $K/K_0$ and a subspace $L\\subset\nD_K$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *The one-step filtration with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fil}}^1D_K=L$ is weakly admissible.*\n\n2.  *$L$ is stable under the cohomological actions of $E$ and $u$, and is Lagrangian for $\\beta$.*\n\n3.  *After extending to an algebraically closed field $\\Omega/K$, one has $\\dim_\\Omega L_\\tau=s$ for every embedding $\\tau:E\\hookrightarrow\\Omega$.*\n\n4.  *The isocrystal, its indicated tensors, and this filtration have finite local field descent.*\n\n*Moreover, geometrically $L$ belongs to the balanced polarized cocharacter orbit. More explicitly, define $$G_D=\\left\\{g\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GSp}}(D,\\beta)\\cap\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{E\\otimes_\\mathbb QK_0}(D):\n \\det_{E\\otimes_\\mathbb QK_0}(g)=c(g)^s\\right\\},$$ where the scalar on the right is viewed in $(E\\otimes_\\mathbb QK_0)^\\times$. The balanced $E$-stable $\\beta$-Lagrangians form a single $G_D(\\Omega)$-orbit. Each is the weight-$1$ space of a cocharacter of $G_{D,\\Omega}$ with weights $0,1$. In particular $L$ belongs to the orbit of any original filtration of this type under a tensor-preserving comparison.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-filtration",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A_0/\\overline{\\mathbb F}_p$ be an abelian variety equipped with a rational action of a CM field $E$ and a polarization whose Rosati involution restricts to conjugation on $E$. More explicitly, define $$G_D=\\left\\{g\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GSp}}(D,\\beta)\\cap\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_{E\\otimes_\\mathbb QK_0}(D):\n \\det_{E\\otimes_\\mathbb QK_0}(g)=c(g)^s\\right\\},$$ where the scalar on the right is viewed in $(E\\otimes_\\mathbb QK_0)^\\times$. The balanced $E$-stable $\\beta$-Lagrangians form a single $G_D(\\Omega)$-orbit. Each is the weight-$1$ space of a cocharacter of $G_{D,\\Omega}$ with weights $0,1$. In particular $L$ belongs to the orbit of any original filtration of this type under a tensor-preserving comparison.",
          "source_id": "PM-001.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $A_0/\\overline{\\mathbb F}_p$ be an abelian variety equipped with a rational action of a CM field $E$ and a polarization whose Rosati involution restricts to conjugation on $E$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a symplectic space of dimension $2m$ over a field $k$ of characteristic zero, and let $W\\subset M$ have dimension $a$. A nonempty open subset of the Lagrangian Grassmannian consists of subspaces $L$ such that $$\\dim(L\\cap W)=\\max(0,a-m).$$ On any fixed symplectic affine big cell, at least one nonzero polynomial of degree at most $m$ has nonvanishing locus contained in this open subset. This degree bound is independent of $W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-filtration-divisor",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D,L$ be as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:filtration\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:filtration\">10</a>, and let $\\psi$ and $U$ denote the homological polarization and the homological action of $u$ on $D_K^\\vee$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a complex abelian variety, let $R\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}^0(B)$ be a rational projector, and suppose that there is an algebra action $E\\longrightarrow R\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}^0(B)R$ whose unit maps to $R$. Let $T=\\operatorname{im}(R)$ in rational first homology, with $\\dim_E T=2s$; the indicated endomorphisms act by zero on $\\ker(R)$. Suppose there is a rational divisor class $D$ on $B$ such that $R^*D=D$ and, after splitting $E$, its alternating form is $$D=\\sum_\\tau D_\\tau,\n \\qquad D_\\tau\\in\\bigwedge\\nolimits^2 T_\\tau^\\vee\\otimes\\mathbb Q(1),$$ with each $D_\\tau$ nondegenerate. Then the rational subspace whose scalar extension is $$\\bigoplus_\\tau\\bigwedge\\nolimits^{2s}T_\\tau^\\vee\\otimes\\mathbb Q(s),$$ extended by zero on the complementary summand, consists of rational Lefschetz classes on $B$.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span",

Let $T=\\operatorname{im}(R)$ in rational first homology, with $\\dim_E T=2s$; the indicated endomorphisms act by zero on $\\ker(R)$. Suppose there is a rational divisor class $D$ on $B$ such that $R^*D=D$ and, after splitting $E$, its alternating form is $$D=\\sum_\\tau D_\\tau,\n \\qquad D_\\tau\\in\\bigwedge\\nolimits^2 T_\\tau^\\vee\\otimes\\mathbb Q(1),$$ with each $D_\\tau$ nondegenerate. Then the rational subspace whose scalar extension is $$\\bigoplus_\\tau\\bigwedge\\nolimits^{2s}T_\\tau^\\vee\\otimes\\mathbb Q(s),$$ extended by zero on the complementary summand, consists of rational Lefschetz classes on $B$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weil-group",
        "exact_quote": "The Hodge homomorphism of $A$ factors through $G$, and its conjugacy class defines a Shimura datum of Hodge type through the displayed embedding. Over an algebraically closed coefficient field, the orbit of the corresponding filtration on $H^1$ consists exactly of the $E$-stable Lagrangians for the dual polarization pairing whose dimension at each embedding of $E$ is $s$. Using a basis of its Tate line, every alternating form in <a href=\"#eq:weil-pure-space\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weil-pure-space\">[eq:weil-pure-space]</a> transforms by $c^s$.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cm-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of CM type with good reduction at $w$, and let $r>0$. For every rational Hodge class $\\gamma\\in H^{2r}(A(\\mathbb C),\\mathbb Q(r))$, there are finitely many abelian varieties $B_i/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$, rational homomorphisms $f_i\\colon A\\to B_i$, and rational Weil classes $\\eta_i\\in H^{2r}(B_i(\\mathbb C),\\mathbb Q(r))$ such that $$\\label{eq:cm-pullback-decomposition}\n                 \\gamma=\\sum_i f_i^*\\eta_i.$$ Each $B_i$ has an action of a CM field $K$ for which $H_1(B_i(\\mathbb C),\\mathbb Q)$ has rank $2r$ over $K$, the Hodge signatures are $(r,r)$ at every embedding of $K$, and a polarization induces complex conjugation on $K$. The equality specializes simultaneously in all the realizations used in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cm-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of CM type with good reduction at $w$, and let $r>0$. The equality specializes simultaneously in all the realizations used in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-001.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-all-hodge-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "For every abelian variety $A/\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$ of dimension $d$ with good reduction $A_0/\\mathbb F$ at $w$, every $0\\leq r\\leq d$, and every rational Hodge class $$\\gamma\\in H^{2r}(A(\\mathbb C),\\mathbb Q(r)),$$ there is a unique class $\\rho_\\gamma\\in\\mathcal R^r(A_0)$ such that $$(\\rho_\\gamma)_\\ell=\\gamma_{0,\\ell}\\quad(\\ell\\ne p),\\qquad\n (\\rho_\\gamma)_p\\otimes 1=\\gamma_{0,p}.$$ The prime-to-$p$ equalities are in $H^{2r}_{\\acute{e}t}(A_0,\\mathbb Q_\\ell(r))$; the crystalline equality is in $$H^{2r}_{\\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\\mathbb F))[1/p](r)\n       \\otimes_{K_0}C_w.$$ The specialization components are those of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. The assertion holds for every prime $p$, including $p=2$, with the prime, place, and theory fixed before $A$ and $\\gamma$ vary.",
        "source_id": "PM-001.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Lefschetz pairing property** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): A tuple $\alpha=(\alpha_v)_v$, with $\alpha_v\in H_v^{2r}(X)(r)$, has the *Lefschetz pairing property* if, for every $\delta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^{\dim X-r}(X)$, the numbers $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_v(\alpha_v\delta_v)$ are the images of one number in $\mathbb Q$.
- **Weil classes** ([source](#stmt-def-weil-space)): The rational space of *Weil classes* associated with $E$ is the subspace $$W_E^s(A)\subset \bigwedge\nolimits_{\mathbb Q}^{2s} V^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q(s) =H^{2s}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(s))$$ whose scalar extension to a splitting field $\Omega$ of $E$ is $$\label{eq:weil-pure-space} W_E^s(A)\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega =\bigoplus_{\tau:E\hookrightarrow\Omega} \bigwedge\nolimits_\Omega^{2s}V_\tau^\vee \otimes\mathbb Q(s)_\Omega.$$ Here each summand is extended by zero on the other eigenspaces. The right side is Galois stable, so it defines the indicated rational subspace.
- **rational Lefschetz space** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Let $\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^r(X)$ be the space of rational polynomial expressions in divisors of codimension $r$, modulo numerical equivalence.
- **simultaneous rational Lefschetz class** ([source](#pm-h-12)): We call the tuple of realizations of a fixed element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^r(X)$ a *simultaneous rational Lefschetz class*.
- **finite local-field descent** ([source](#sec:transport)): We say that filtered isocrystal data have *finite local-field descent* if they are obtained by scalar extension from an isocrystal with its tensors over the maximal unramified subfield $H_0$ of a finite extension $H/\mathbb Q_p$, together with a filtration over $H$.
- **tensor isogeny class** ([source](#pm-h-16)): The *tensor isogeny class* $\mathcal I_x$ consists of the points $y\in\mathcal S_K(\mathbb F)$ for which a single quasi-isogeny $C_x\to C_y$ preserves $s_{\alpha,0}$ and every $s_{\alpha,\ell}$ for $\ell\ne p$.
- **weak admissibility** ([source](#sec:filtration)): Weak admissibility means that $t_H(D)=t_N(D)$ and $t_H(P)\leq t_N(P)$ for every isocrystal subobject $P$ over $K_0$.
- **rational projector** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A rational projector here means an idempotent in the rational endomorphism algebra.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison): Cite the absolute-Hodge theorem and the p-adic comparison theorem, then extend to finite extensions of the base field. -> compatibility of all realizations with the comparison isomorphisms
- **step-pairing-functorial** [claim](#stmt-lem-pairing-functorial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pairing-functorial): Solve one invertible rational linear system from the projection formula to show pushforward of a Lefschetz class is simultaneous rational. -> closure of the pairing property under rational pullback
- **step-lift-hom** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift-hom) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift-hom): Use full faithfulness of filtered Dieudonné modules, Tate extension, Serre–Tate deformation and algebraization. -> unique lift of the rational homomorphism
- **step-auxiliary-datum** [claim](#stmt-lem-auxiliary-datum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-auxiliary-datum): Choose a totally real field splitting the group at p, form the equal-multiplier fiber product, and check strong admissibility explicitly. -> auxiliary Shimura datum with quasi-split local group
- **step-admissible-realization** [claim](#stmt-lem-admissible-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-admissible-realization): Find a framed point over the prescribed period via an étale period map, then apply integral uniformization of the local Shimura variety. -> abelian lift with marked quasi-isogeny realizing the filtration
- **step-transport** [claim](#stmt-prop-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-admissible-realization-2): Build a trace tensor, choose a CM lift or a prescribed-filtration lift, and normalize local similitude scalars by a ratio of rational intersection numbers. -> quasi-isogeny and Hodge class with equal pullback in all realizations
- **step-cm-transport** [claim](#stmt-cor-cm-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cm-transport): Apply the first alternative of the transport proposition to the Mumford–Tate datum. -> CM transport of the Hodge class
- **step-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-lem-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-avoidance): Pick a root of a degree-q polynomial of high ramification, and substitute powers so distinct monomials stay distinct. -> one point avoiding all bounded-degree polynomials
- **step-lagrangian-intersection** [claim](#stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection): Dimension count on an incidence variety, then a minor of a matrix affine in the symmetric-matrix coordinates. -> nonzero bounded-degree polynomial enforcing minimal intersection
- **step-filtration-construction** [claim](#stmt-thm-filtration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection-2): Reduce weak admissibility to stable subobjects by a supermodularity and maximizer argument, split into symplectic blocks, and choose Lagrangians with minimal intersections via the avoidance lemma. -> weakly admissible stable Lagrangian filtration with finite descent
- **step-filtration-admissible** [claim](#stmt-cor-filtration-admissible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-filtration-admissible): Apply the weakly-admissible-implies-admissible theorem with zero monodromy. -> crystalline representation
- **step-filtration-divisor** [claim](#stmt-cor-filtration-divisor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-filtration-divisor): Use the annihilator of L, stable under u, to place the divisor form in the kernel of the restriction to the quotient. -> divisor filtration condition
- **step-weil-orthogonality** [claim](#stmt-lem-weil-orthogonality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weil-orthogonality): Prove the contrapositive: a nonzero pairing gives a nondegenerate divisor form on an eigenspace, a rational element of the endomorphism space with that property, and then invertibility. -> vanishing of all complementary pairings
- **step-weil-divisor-span** [claim](#stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span): Extract coefficients of a polynomial in the eigenvalue coordinates and use Zariski density of rational points. -> determinant classes as rational Lefschetz classes
- **step-weil-group** [claim](#stmt-lem-weil-group) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weil-group): Compute the group over an algebraically closed field, check Shimura datum axioms, and compute the transitive action on balanced Lagrangians. -> Hodge-type datum and filtration orbit
- **step-weil-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-prop-weil) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weil-group-2): Vanishing case from the orthogonality lemma; otherwise build the filtration, transport it, lift the projector, action and divisor, and use the divisor span lemma. -> Weil classes satisfy the Lefschetz pairing property
- **step-cm-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-cm-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cm-reduction): Decompose into eigenspace components, build balanced factors by rational idempotents on powers, equip them with compatible polarizations, and sum pullback spaces. -> decomposition into pullbacks of Weil classes
- **step-cm-pairing** [claim](#stmt-cor-cm-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cm-pairing): Combine the Weil reduction, the Weil dichotomy and closure under pullback and linear combinations. -> pairing property for CM classes
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cm-pairing-2): Treat extreme codimensions directly, then combine CM transport, the CM pairing property and pullback closure. -> one rational number in all realizations
- **step-all-hodge-fixed-theory** [claim](#stmt-cor-all-hodge-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-all-hodge-specialization): Pull back the class of the CM variety in the fixed good theory along a homomorphism, clearing a denominator, and use injectivity for uniqueness. -> unique class in the fixed theory
- **step-algebraic-specialization** [claim](#stmt-cor-algebraic-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-algebraic-specialization): Represent the CM class by an algebraic cycle using the CM Hodge theorem, specialize it, and pull back by Chow pullback. -> rational algebraic cycle on the reduction

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-common-rational-pairing**: For a rational Hodge class on an abelian variety with good reduction, the pairing of its specialization with any complementary product of Cartier divisors on the reduction is one rational number, the same in every prime-to-p realization and in crystalline cohomology.
- **inv-lefschetz-pairing-property**: The Lefschetz pairing property says that traces of a realization tuple against every complementary rational Lefschetz class are images of a single rational number.
- **inv-pairing-stable-under-pullback**: The Lefschetz pairing property is preserved by pullback along a rational homomorphism of abelian varieties.
- **inv-weil-orthogonality-branch**: When the space of rational endomorphisms skew-commuting with the CM action has no invertible element, specialized Weil classes pair to zero with all complementary Lefschetz classes in each realization.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-abelian-varieties**: Applying the rational pairing conclusion to general smooth projective varieties that are not abelian. -> The argument rests on structure special to abelian varieties, so nothing is established for other varieties.
- **anti-bad-reduction-places**: Specializing at a place where the abelian variety does not have good reduction. -> No smooth proper special fiber with crystalline cohomology is available, so the specialized class is undefined.
- **anti-cycle-representation**: Reading the main rationality statement as saying the specialized Hodge class is represented by an algebraic cycle. -> The rationality statement alone concerns pairings with divisor products, not cycle representation.
- **anti-cohomological-membership-as-conjecture**: Treating membership of the specialized class in the fixed good theory as a proof of the Hodge or Tate conjecture. -> Membership is a cohomological statement and yields no algebraic cycle.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence and equality statements in cohomology of abelian varieties: the main theorem asserts that a rational number exists and is common to all realizations, and the supporting results transport classes by quasi-isogenies and choose filtrations by genericity and deformation theory over p-adic fields. None gives a finite procedure with inputs and outputs that could be run and checked by an independent oracle; the proofs invoke integral Shimura models, comparison theorems and Zariski density, which are not finite computations.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence and rationality statement about cohomology classes on abelian varieties. It gives no finite procedure that could be shipped as a software component. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no dynamics, state space or simulation model. Its content is arithmetic-geometric cohomology. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper states no procedure a verification tool could execute. The proofs use non-constructive choices such as points over finite extensions and existence of lifts. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bounded-degree avoidance lemma is an existence statement. It chooses a point over a ramified extension and gives no complexity-bearing algorithm for the whole result. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data layout or storage structure is proposed. The objects are cohomology spaces and Lefschetz algebras. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no numeric kernel or circuit-level content that hardware could accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Abelian varieties appear only through their cohomology and specialization. No protocol, hardness assumption or security claim is made. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning, data or statistical content in the paper. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimisation problem is posed. The weak-admissibility inequalities are structural conditions, not an optimisation target. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no notion of nodes, messages or consensus. Compatibility across realizations is a statement about cohomology theories. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is pure arithmetic geometry. Its only reuse is as a theoretical input to other theorems on Hodge classes and Tate classes, which is not an engineering application. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-good-reduction-place** (hypothesis): The main theorem and its corollaries are stated only for an abelian variety that has good reduction at the fixed place. Without good reduction there is no smooth proper special fiber, so the specialized class and the crystalline realization are undefined. Blocked use: ['Applying the rational pairing conclusion at a place of bad reduction', 'Specializing a Hodge class without first passing to a finite extension with good reduction']
- **limit-rational-hodge-class** (hypothesis): The class must be a rational Hodge class in the rational cohomology of the complex points of the abelian variety. Arbitrary rational or non-Hodge classes are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the pairing statement to an arbitrary cohomology class that is not a rational Hodge class']
- **limit-divisor-products-only** (hypothesis): Theorem 1 gives a common rational number only for pairings against products of Cartier divisors on the reduction, that is, rational Lefschetz classes. Pairings against arbitrary algebraic cycles need the separate algebraic specialization corollary, which uses the CM Hodge theorem. Blocked use: ['Concluding rationality of pairings with arbitrary algebraic cycles from Theorem 1 alone', 'Assuming the main proof depends on the CM Hodge theorem']
- **limit-no-cycle-from-pairing** (formal_scope): The rationality statement is isolated from the stronger problem of representing the Hodge class by an algebraic cycle. Membership of the specialized class in the fixed good theory is cohomological and neither proves the Hodge conjecture nor the Tate conjecture. Blocked use: ['Reading Theorem 1 as producing an algebraic cycle', 'Using membership in the fixed good theory as a proof of the Hodge or Tate conjecture']
- **limit-fixed-place-no-comparison** (edge_case): The common number q is attached to fixed data: the prime, the place and the class. No comparison between different reduction places is made, and the representing cycle is not asserted to be unique or to lift to the generic fiber. Blocked use: ['Treating q as an invariant across different reduction places', 'Lifting the representing cycle to the original generic fiber']
- **limit-abelian-varieties-only** (hypothesis): The argument uses structure special to abelian varieties: absolute Hodge classes, CM transport and Weil classes. Nothing is claimed for other smooth projective varieties. Blocked use: ['Applying the rationality conclusion to non-abelian varieties such as K3 surfaces or Calabi-Yau threefolds']
- **limit-weil-dichotomy** (edge_case): For balanced Weil classes the argument splits according to whether the space of symmetric endomorphisms skew-commuting with the CM field contains an invertible element. The filtration and lifting stage is needed only in the invertible case. Blocked use: ['Assuming a divisor from an invertible endomorphism exists when none is available', 'Applying the filtration and lifting construction when the space has no invertible element']
- **limit-filtration-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The filtration is produced by choosing a point over a finite extension that avoids the zero loci of bounded-degree polynomials. The realization step picks a point of an etale rigid space over a finite extension. Neither step is an explicit procedure. Blocked use: ['Claiming an algorithm or complexity bound for constructing the filtration', 'Treating the choice of lift as computable']
- **limit-avoidance-coefficient-field** (edge_case): The avoidance lemma concerns polynomials over the fixed field K_1. It would fail if the enlarged field were used as the coefficient field. Blocked use: ['Applying the avoidance lemma to polynomials with coefficients in the enlarged field']
- **limit-lift-hom-filtration** (hypothesis): A special-fiber rational homomorphism lifts only when its contravariant crystalline map preserves the Hodge filtrations. An arbitrary map of p-divisible groups is not asserted to be a rational algebraic map. Blocked use: ['Lifting a special-fiber homomorphism whose crystalline map does not preserve the Hodge filtrations', 'Lifting arbitrary linear maps of p-divisible groups as algebraic maps']
- **limit-auxiliary-not-generic-fiber** (counterexample_scope): The CM auxiliary variety may have larger dimension and the map is between special fibers only. No same-dimensional CM lift or map between generic fibers is supplied. Blocked use: ['Assuming a quasi-isogeny between the generic fibers of the original and auxiliary varieties', 'Assuming the CM auxiliary has the same dimension as the original variety']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Milne's rationality conjecture for abelian varieties, including residue characteristic 2. After good reduction, the pairing of a rational Hodge class with any complementary product of divisor classes on the reduction is the same rational number in every prime-to-<i>p</i> realization and in crystalline cohomology. Using the Hodge theorem for CM abelian varieties, we also show that every such specialized Hodge class is represented by a single rational algebraic cycle in all these realizations.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

Suppose an abelian variety has good reduction at a prime. A rational algebraic cycle on the variety specializes to a cycle on the reduction. Its intersection with any complementary product of divisors has a rational degree, and every cycle-class realization computes that same number. Milne’s rationality conjecture asks for this conclusion when the original class is only known to be a rational Hodge class. We prove it for every residue characteristic.

The distinction matters because good reduction can acquire divisors that do not lift to the original variety. Deligne’s absolute-Hodge theorem gives compatible realizations of the Hodge class, but does not by itself identify its pairing with these new divisors as the degree of a rational cycle. The conjecture isolates this rationality question from the stronger problem of representing the Hodge class by an algebraic cycle.

Fix $\overline{\mathbb Q}\subset\mathbb C$, a prime $p$, and a $p$-adic place $w$ of $\overline{\mathbb Q}$. Write $$\mathbb F=\overline{\mathbb F}_p,\qquad
 K_0=\operatorname{Frac}W(\mathbb F),\qquad
 C_w=\widehat{\overline{\mathbb Q}}_w.$$ We regard $K_0$ and an algebraic closure of it as subfields of $C_w$. Good reduction means good reduction after descent to a number field and finite extension at the chosen place.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of dimension $d$ with good reduction $A_0/\mathbb F$ at $w$. Let $0\leq r\leq d$, and let $$\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$$ be a rational Hodge class. For every list of Cartier divisors $D_1,\ldots,D_{d-r}$ on $A_0$, there is a number $q\in\mathbb Q$ such that $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_\ell\bigl(\gamma_{0,\ell}
       c_{1,\ell}(D_1)\cdots c_{1,\ell}(D_{d-r})\bigr)&=q
       &&\text{in }\mathbb Q_\ell\quad(\ell\ne p),\\
 \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_p\bigl(\gamma_{0,p}
       c_{1,\mathrm{cris}}(D_1)\cdots
       c_{1,\mathrm{cris}}(D_{d-r})\bigr)&=q
       &&\text{in }C_w.
\end{aligned}$$ Here $\gamma_{0,\ell}$ is the smooth proper specialization of the $\ell$-adic realization, and $\gamma_{0,p}$ is the de Rham–crystalline specialization in $$H^{2r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p](r)
       \otimes_{K_0}C_w.$$ The traces use the top Tate twist and send the class of a zero-cycle to its degree. If $r=d$, the divisor product is $1$.*

</div>

By linearity the same conclusion holds for every rational Lefschetz class of complementary codimension, that is, every rational linear combination of products of divisors. The number $q$ is common to all realizations for the fixed data; no comparison between different reduction places is required.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Origins and consequences

Milne introduced a weak-rationality formulation while studying the reduction of Shimura varieties and the Langlands–Rapoport conjecture <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Milne2000](#ref-Milne2000), Conjecture 6.1(A)]. It asks that a specialized CM Hodge class whose components lie in the local spans of divisor products in every realization arise from one rational Lefschetz class. The pairing formulation appears in his AIM notes and his study of rational Tate classes <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[MilneAIM2007](#ref-MilneAIM2007), Section 10], <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), Conjecture 4.1]. A previous positive case is a CM abelian variety with simple ordinary reduction, together with all its powers: in that case every divisor class on the reduction lifts <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), Example 4.2].

The broader purpose is to obtain rational cohomological correspondences for abelian motives in characteristic $p$, compatible with reduction from characteristic zero <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Milne2000](#ref-Milne2000), Sections 5–6]; see also <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Milne2025](#ref-Milne2025), Introduction and Section 2]. For $\ell\ne p$, Tate classes are twisted $\ell$-adic classes fixed by a power of Frobenius; there is a corresponding $\mathbb Q_p$-algebra of crystalline Tate classes. A *good theory of rational Tate classes* chooses one graded $\mathbb Q$-algebra on each variety whose scalar extensions give these Tate algebras. The theory is stable under pullback and pushforward, contains divisor classes, and contains specializations of CM Hodge classes <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), Definitions 2.1 and 3.1]. These are cohomological correspondences; their definition does not assert that they are algebraic.

Milne proved that the rationality assertion for all CM abelian varieties is equivalent to the existence of such a good theory on varieties over $\mathbb F$ whose connected components are products of abelian varieties and projective spaces <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), Theorem 4.5]. Our proof also expresses each specialized Hodge class as the pullback, along a rational homomorphism of special fibers, of a specialized Hodge class from a CM abelian variety. This extends the theory’s specialization property beyond the CM case. In Corollary <a href="#cor:all-hodge-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:all-hodge-specialization">23</a>, every rational Hodge class on a good-reduction abelian variety specializes to a unique class in one fixed good theory. Its crystalline component gives the stated specialization after extension to $C_w$.

A distinct additional input gives algebraicity on the special fiber. Combining the same transport with the Hodge theorem for CM abelian varieties <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[OpenAICMHodge2026](#ref-OpenAICMHodge2026), Theorem 1.1], Corollary <a href="#cor:algebraic-specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:algebraic-specialization">24</a> represents each specialized Hodge class by one rational algebraic cycle in all the stated realizations. Its pairing with any complementary algebraic cycle is therefore a rational intersection number. This consequence uses the CM Hodge theorem; the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is independent of it.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The method and its antecedents

Three foundational results make this question accessible. Deligne proved that every Hodge class on an abelian variety is absolute Hodge <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Deligne1982](#ref-Deligne1982), Main Theorem 2.11]. Blasius established its compatibility with $p$-adic comparison <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Blasius1994](#ref-Blasius1994), Theorems 0.3 and 5.3]; Moonen presents the Blasius–Wintenberger comparison theorem, with Ogus’s simplification, in the perfect-residue-field form needed for the local auxiliary lifts below <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Moonen1998](#ref-Moonen1998), Theorem 5.6.3]. Milne’s theory of Lefschetz classes supplies a common rational divisor algebra with perfect complementary pairing, independent of the Weil cohomology <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Milne1999](#ref-Milne1999), Section 5].

The determinant classes used in the proof belong to the theory of Weil classes developed by Weil, Deligne and André; see <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Deligne1982](#ref-Deligne1982), Section 4 and Endnotes M.10–M.12]. André’s refinement of the CM reduction expresses Hodge classes by pullbacks from varieties of split Weil type <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Andre1992](#ref-Andre1992)]; a detailed account is <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Milne2020](#ref-Milne2020), Theorem 1]. We prove directly the balanced form of that reduction needed here in Lemma <a href="#lem:cm-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cm-reduction">21</a>. Balanced signatures assert equal Hodge multiplicities at conjugate embeddings; split Weil type also requires the associated Hermitian form to have a half-dimensional isotropic subspace. André’s later theory of motivated cycles establishes a broader unconditional motivic setting for abelian Hodge classes <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Andre1996](#ref-Andre1996), Theorem 0.6.2], without identifying them with algebraic cycles.

For an imaginary quadratic field, Markman has proved algebraicity of Weil classes on abelian fourfolds of Weil type and on abelian sixfolds of split Weil type <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Markman2026](#ref-Markman2026), Theorem 1.2]. These are characteristic-zero algebraicity results. The pairing argument here applies in arbitrary dimension and begins only with the Hodge class and its good reduction.

There is also a direct antecedent for the lifting strategy. Milne asked whether a specialized CM Hodge class and a divisor could be lifted together, up to isogeny, to a CM variety, and explained the resulting rationality implication <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[MilneAIM2007](#ref-MilneAIM2007), Question 10.6 and Proposition 10.7]. His current account gives a conditional criterion through weak lifting of Lefschetz classes on CM Weil triples and describes the reduction from general Hodge classes through CM varieties to Weil classes <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Milne2025](#ref-Milne2025), Introduction, Question 2.17, and Theorem 2.18]. Our proof follows this general route, using two auxiliary varieties with different purposes. The first is CM; the second realizes a prescribed admissible filtration over a local field and need not be CM. The necessary realization machinery comes from Grothendieck–Serre–Tate deformation theory, Tate’s extension theorem, and the integral-model and local-Shimura results of Kisin–Zhou and Pappas–Rapoport <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Grothendieck1970](#ref-Grothendieck1970), [Tate1967](#ref-Tate1967), [KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), [PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024)]. For the prescribed filtration, we choose a framed local Shimura point over its admissible period and use integral uniformization; the required uniformization condition is supplied by <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[GleasonLimXu2026](#ref-GleasonLimXu2026), Corollary 6.3]. We state the precise inputs where they enter and prove the connecting arguments for the prescribed flag.

The contribution along this route is to construct a filtration that preserves the extra divisor and then obtain a rational divisor identity on its lift. A separate normalization by rational intersection numbers makes the tensor transports simultaneous in all realizations. Kisin–Zhou’s compatibility theorem concerns Weil–Deligne representations across coefficient fields <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Theorem 1.2]; the additional issue here is the rational value of a Hodge–divisor intersection.

The key step is a dichotomy for balanced Weil classes: either all the complementary Lefschetz pairings vanish, or the specialized class is itself a rational polynomial in divisors, simultaneously in every realization.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof overview

The proof has four stages. The order below describes the deduction; Sections <a href="#sec:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transport">3</a>–<a href="#sec:weil" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weil">5</a> establish its reusable constructions before Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">6</a> assembles them.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### 1. Transport to a CM variety.

Corollary <a href="#cor:cm-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cm-transport">9</a> constructs a CM abelian variety $C/\overline{\mathbb Q}$, a quasi-isogeny $a_{\mathrm{CM}}:A_0^n\to C_0$, and a rational Hodge class $\kappa$ such that $$\gamma_0=(a_{\mathrm{CM}}j)^*\kappa_0,$$ where $j$ includes the first factor. This is one rational algebraic homomorphism on the special fibers and one equality in every realization. Kisin–Zhou’s special-point theorem supplies the CM lift <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Theorem 2.2.7]. The local tensors initially determine similitude scalars; a ratio of rational divisor intersections shows that all those scalars are the same rational number. This normalization is what transports the original rational class, rather than a separate local multiple in each cohomology theory.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### 2. Reduce CM classes to balanced Weil classes.

Lemma <a href="#lem:cm-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cm-reduction">21</a> constructs rational-idempotent factors of actual powers of $C$. Their homology has rank $2s$ over a CM field $E$, with signatures $(s,s)$ at every embedding, and their polarizations induce conjugation on $E$. Their Weil classes are the rational forms whose components, after splitting $E$, are top alternating forms on individual $E$-eigenspaces. Pullbacks of these Hodge classes span the given CM Hodge class over $\mathbb Q$. Good reduction is retained. Having one rational value for each complementary Lefschetz pairing is preserved by rational pullback (Lemma <a href="#lem:pairing-functorial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pairing-functorial">4</a>). It therefore remains to prove that assertion for balanced Weil classes.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### 3. Find a divisor and a compatible filtration.

For a balanced Weil variety $Z$, let $\dagger$ denote Rosati on its reduction and consider $$\mathcal U=\{u\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(Z_0):u^\dagger=u,\quad
                         ue=\bar e u\text{ for all }e\in E\}.$$ Proposition <a href="#prop:weil" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:weil">17</a> proves a dichotomy. If $\mathcal U$ has no invertible element, every complementary Lefschetz pairing of a specialized Weil class is zero. If it has an invertible element, the specialized Weil class is itself a simultaneous rational Lefschetz class: one rational polynomial in divisors gives its class in every realization. For the second branch, such a $u$ supplies a divisor $D^u$ whose alternating form is nondegenerate on each $E$-eigenspace. Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a> constructs a weakly admissible balanced filtration on the fixed crystalline module that preserves $E$, $u$, and the polarization. Generic transversality has a classical role in admissible-filtration constructions <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[FontaineRapoport2005](#ref-FontaineRapoport2005), Theorem 1 and Lemmas 1.1–1.2]. Here bounded-degree avoidance makes all the constrained choices over one finite extension, and a maximality argument reduces the weak-admissibility test to subobjects stable under the endomorphisms.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### 4. Lift the divisor and extract a rational identity.

The second case of Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a> realizes this flag on a local auxiliary abelian variety $B$, with a marked quasi-isogeny $a_{\mathrm{fl}}:Z_0^m\to B_0$. The prescribed flag makes the first-summand projector, its algebraic $E$-action, and the transported divisor filtered. These specified algebraic maps lift to $B$. After choosing an abstract complex realization of $B$, polynomial coefficient extraction expresses the relevant rational Betti Weil class as a rational linear combination of divisor products (Lemma <a href="#lem:weil-divisor-span" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weil-divisor-span">19</a>). The same rational identity specializes in every realization. Pullback returns it to $Z_0$, completing the case of an invertible element in the dichotomy and, through the first two stages, the main theorem.

Figure <a href="#fig:two-transports" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:two-transports">1</a> separates the two lifts and their special-fiber markings. Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> fixes comparisons, traces, and rational pullback. Section <a href="#sec:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transport">3</a> proves both transport alternatives; Section <a href="#sec:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:filtration">4</a> constructs the prescribed flag; Section <a href="#sec:weil" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weil">5</a> proves the balanced-Weil dichotomy; and Section <a href="#sec:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reduction">6</a> proves the CM reduction and finishes the argument.

<figure id="fig:two-transports">

<figcaption>The two uses of transport. Each lower horizontal arrow is one actual quasi-isogeny preserving the specified tensors in all realizations; the vertical arrows denote good reduction. There is no claimed map between the generic fibers. The upper construction starts from the original variety. The lower construction is applied separately to a balanced Weil variety $Z$ arising in the CM reduction; its lift $B$ realizes the chosen filtration $ℱ_{Z}^{⊕m}$ under $a_{fl}^{*}$. The CM and prescribed-filtration auxiliaries need not coincide.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-two-transports"></a>

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Comparisons and rational Lefschetz classes

We first put the realizations in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> in a common notation and formulate its conclusion as a property of cohomology classes. The essential functorial fact is that this property is preserved by rational pullback. We also record the criterion that will lift an additional divisor once a suitable Hodge filtration has been realized.

<a id="eq:rational-pullback"></a>
We work in the category of abelian varieties up to isogeny. Thus $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^0(X,Y)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(X,Y)\otimes_\mathbb Z\mathbb Q$, and divisors may have rational coefficients. Pullback by a rational homomorphism is defined on $H^1$ and extended to its exterior algebra. If $Nf$ is an actual homomorphism, then on $H^{2r}$ $$\label{eq:rational-pullback}
 f^*=N^{-2r}(Nf)^*.$$ This convention is the same in all realizations.

For an abelian variety $X/\mathbb F$, set $$k_\ell=\mathbb Q_\ell\quad(\ell\ne p),\qquad k_p=C_w,$$ and write $H_v^{2r}(X)(r)$ for its $\ell$-adic cohomology if $v=\ell\ne p$, and for crystalline cohomology extended to $C_w$ if $v=p$. Cup products will usually be written without a cup symbol. All trace maps and divisor classes have their cycle-class normalizations. The Betti Tate line $\mathbb Q(r)$ supplies the twists in the other realizations through comparison. Thus $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_v$ is the trace on top cohomology with its top Tate twist and sends the class of a zero-cycle to its degree. For a good-reduction lift, $\gamma_{0,\ell}$ denotes smooth proper specialization of its absolute-Hodge realization, and $\gamma_{0,p}$ denotes de Rham–crystalline specialization extended from $K_0$ to $C_w$. We keep first crystalline cohomology over $K_0$ when constructing filtrations, and specify each extension of the filtration field separately.

We use the exterior-algebra description of abelian cohomology and faithfulness of rational homomorphisms on $H^1$; see <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[MilneAV](#ref-MilneAV), Theorems 12.5 and 15.1] for the prime-to-characteristic realizations. The uniform Weil-cohomology formalism, including crystalline cohomology, exterior powers, cycle-normalized traces and the projection formula, is set out in <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Milne1999](#ref-Milne1999), Section 1 and Appendix, Proposition (A.2)]. Smooth proper specialization away from $p$ is <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[MilneLEC](#ref-MilneLEC), Theorem 20.4].

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Comparison of Hodge tensors

<a id="stmt-lem-comparison"></a>
<div id="lem:comparison" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *For an abelian variety with good reduction, the realizations of an absolute Hodge class are compatible with the de Rham and crystalline comparison isomorphisms. In particular, the $p$-adic crystalline tensor obtained from its $p$-adic étale realization agrees, after extension of scalars, with the de Rham–crystalline realization in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. These compatibilities respect tensor operations, duals, Tate twists, divisor classes, and rational homomorphisms.*

*The same assertion applies to auxiliary abelian varieties with good reduction over a finite extension of $K_0$, using a complex embedding of an algebraic closure of their field of definition.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* For abelian varieties over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, Deligne’s absolute-Hodge theorem <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Deligne1982](#ref-Deligne1982), Main Theorem 2.11] and Blasius’s comparison theorem <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Blasius1994](#ref-Blasius1994), Theorems 0.3 and 5.3] give the assertion. The compatibility of the crystalline comparison with the de Rham comparison is part of <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Blasius1994](#ref-Blasius1994), 5.1]. The formulation for a complete mixed-characteristic discrete valuation ring with perfect residue field is given in <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Moonen1998](#ref-Moonen1998), Theorem 5.6.3]; the paragraph following that theorem explains removal of the number-field hypothesis. A finite extension of $K_0$ is within that scope. The stated functorialities are those of the comparison isomorphisms. ◻

</details>

An abstract complex embedding used here need not be continuous. To see that it can be chosen compatibly with $\overline{\mathbb Q}\subset\mathbb C$, take a transcendence basis of $\overline{K_0}/\overline{\mathbb Q}$, embed it in a transcendence basis of $\mathbb C/\overline{\mathbb Q}$, and extend algebraically. The cardinalities permit this construction. No descent of a general auxiliary lift to $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ is inferred.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### The common rational Lefschetz space

Let $\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^r(X)$ be the space of rational polynomial expressions in divisors of codimension $r$, modulo numerical equivalence. We use the following established properties:

1.  its cycle map is injective, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^r(X)\otimes_\mathbb Qk_v$ is the divisor-generated subspace of $H_v^{2r}(X)(r)$;

2.  intersection and degree give a perfect pairing $$\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^r(X)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^{\dim X-r}(X)\longrightarrow\mathbb Q;$$

3.  pushforward by a homomorphism of abelian varieties preserves the divisor-generated subspaces in each realization;

4.  hard Lefschetz and its inverse restrict to these subspaces.

These are <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Milne1999](#ref-Milne1999), Proposition 5.2, Corollaries 5.3 and 5.5, Theorem 5.9], with the author’s correction to the proof of Theorem 5.9. Crystalline cohomology is included. Extension of its coefficient field to $C_w$ preserves these statements. We call the tuple of realizations of a fixed element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^r(X)$ a *simultaneous rational Lefschetz class*.
