<a id="stmt-auto-definition-1"></a>
<div class="definition">

**Definition 3**. A tuple $\alpha=(\alpha_v)_v$, with $\alpha_v\in H_v^{2r}(X)(r)$, has the *Lefschetz pairing property* if, for every $\delta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^{\dim X-r}(X)$, the numbers $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_v(\alpha_v\delta_v)$ are the images of one number in $\mathbb Q$.

</div>

Simultaneous rational algebraic classes have this property, by the intersection formula. The property is closed under rational linear combinations. We shall need the following additional closure property.

<a id="stmt-lem-pairing-functorial"></a>
<div id="lem:pairing-functorial" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *If $\alpha$ has the Lefschetz pairing property on $Y$ and $f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^0(X,Y)$, then $f^*\alpha$ has that property on $X$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-pairing-functorial"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-pairing-functorial</summary>

*Proof.* If $r>\dim Y$, the input class is zero; if $r>\dim X$, its pullback is zero. We may therefore suppose that $f$ is an actual homomorphism and $r\leq\min(\dim X,\dim Y)$. For $\delta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^{\dim X-r}(X)$, the class $f_*\delta_v$ lies in the Lefschetz subspace of codimension $\dim Y-r$ in every realization. Choose rational bases $a_i$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^{\dim Y-r}(Y)$ and $b_j$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Lef}}^r(Y)$. The matrix $M_{ij}=\deg(a_i b_j)$ is an invertible rational matrix. If $f_*\delta_v=\sum_i c_{i,v}(a_i)_v$, the projection formula gives $$\sum_i c_{i,v}M_{ij}=\deg(\delta f^*b_j).$$ The right side is a rational number independent of $v$. Solving this one rational system shows that $c_{i,v}=c_i\in\mathbb Q$ for every $v$. Thus $f_*\delta$ is simultaneous rational Lefschetz. Applying the projection formula once more proves the assertion. For rational $f$, use <a href="#eq:rational-pullback" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rational-pullback">[eq:rational-pullback]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Lifting a prescribed rational homomorphism

The second transport construction will produce an abelian lift with a chosen Hodge filtration. The following criterion then lifts the algebraic projector, field action, and divisor already specified on its special fiber. Its input is a rational algebraic homomorphism, rather than an arbitrary map between cohomology spaces.

In the next lemma $R$ is a complete mixed-characteristic discrete valuation ring finite over $W(\mathbb F)$, with fraction field $K$. For an abelian scheme $\mathcal X/R$, the Hodge filtration is regarded as a filtration of $H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(X_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p]\otimes_{K_0}K$.

<a id="stmt-lem-lift-hom"></a>
<div id="lem:lift-hom" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *Let $\mathcal X,\mathcal Y$ be abelian schemes over $R$, with special fibers $X_0,Y_0$, and let $f_0\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^0(X_0,Y_0)$. If its contravariant crystalline map preserves the Hodge filtrations, then $f_0$ lifts uniquely to a rational homomorphism $\mathcal X\to\mathcal Y$.*

*In particular, a symmetric rational homomorphism $X_0\to X_0^\vee$ with this property lifts to a rational divisor class on the generic fiber. Algebraic identities among finitely many such lifted maps remain valid.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-lift-hom"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-lift-hom</summary>

*Proof.* The crystalline map already commutes with Frobenius. Full faithfulness for rational filtered Dieudonné modules gives a rational morphism of the associated $p$-divisible groups; see <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Grothendieck1970](#ref-Grothendieck1970)] and the perfect-residue-field formulation in <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[IllusiePisa](#ref-IllusiePisa), 4.2(v)]. Equivalently, crystalline comparison first gives the rational map on Tate modules. Multiply by an integer $N$ that clears both its Tate-lattice denominator and the denominator of $f_0$. Tate’s extension theorem <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[Tate1967](#ref-Tate1967), Theorem 4] extends the resulting integral generic-fiber map of $p$-divisible groups over $R$. Faithfulness of the special-fiber Dieudonné functor identifies its reduction with the map induced by $Nf_0$. Here equality after inverting $p$ is already equality of integral maps, because the integral Dieudonné modules are torsion free. The integer $N$ is fixed for the remainder of the construction.

The specified-map form of Serre–Tate deformation theory now gives a lift over each $R/\mathfrak m_R^n$ <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Grothendieck1970](#ref-Grothendieck1970), p. 432]. One can also deduce it from the fixed-object formulation <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[IllusiePisa](#ref-IllusiePisa), Theorem 2.3.3] as follows. On $X_0\times Y_0$ form the shear $s_0(x,y)=(x,y+Nf_0(x))$. The lifted $p$-divisible-group map gives the same shear on the product $p$-divisible group. View the existing product deformation with two special-fiber markings differing by $s_0$. Full faithfulness lifts the shear to an algebraic isomorphism between these marked deformations; its second component restricted to $X$ lifts $Nf_0$. Uniqueness makes these maps compatible at every finite order.

Formal existence algebraizes the resulting formal map <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[StacksProject](#ref-StacksProject), Tag 0A42]: its source is proper over the complete noetherian ring $R$, and its target is separated and of finite type. Dividing by the fixed $N$ gives the desired rational lift. Rigidity gives uniqueness and injectivity of specialization on rational homomorphisms <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[MilneAV](#ref-MilneAV), Proposition 20.1 and Corollary 20.2].

If $f_0$ is symmetric, injectivity shows that its lift is symmetric. The usual identification $$\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(X_K)\otimes\mathbb Q
   \simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(X_K,X_K^\vee)^{\mathrm{sym}}\otimes\mathbb Q$$ gives the rational divisor class, after a finite extension if necessary <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[MilneAV](#ref-MilneAV), Proposition 17.2]. Finally an identity between the maps specializes to the given identity, so injectivity proves it upstairs as well. ◻

</details>

Only prescribed rational algebraic maps on the special fiber are being lifted in Lemma <a href="#lem:lift-hom" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lift-hom">5</a>. An arbitrary $\mathbb Q_p$-linear map of $p$-divisible groups is not asserted to be a rational algebraic map. We also use the extension of generic-fiber homomorphisms between abelian schemes over a discrete valuation ring: their Néron mapping property extends such a homomorphism uniquely over the base <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[Conrad2003](#ref-Conrad2003), Definition 5.1 and Example 5.3]. Clearing one rational denominator therefore makes specialization of these maps well defined.

<a id="sec:transport"></a>
### Transport through Hodge-type integral models

After taking a power of the original abelian variety, we transport a rational Hodge class to an auxiliary variety by one quasi-isogeny on special fibers, with the same equality in every realization. The auxiliary variety can be chosen either of CM type or with a prescribed admissible Hodge filtration. These choices serve different parts of the proof, but share the tensor construction and the rational normalization in Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a> below.

<a id="eq:transport-multiplier"></a>
Throughout this section, a polarization on a rational homology space is written as a rational alternating form, using the fixed Betti trivialization of the Tate line. If its similitude character is $c$, an alternating form $\xi:V^{2r}\to\mathbb Q$ of multiplier $c^r$ means that $$\xi(gv_1,\ldots,gv_{2r})=c(g)^r\xi(v_1,\ldots,v_{2r}).
 \label{eq:transport-multiplier}$$ Such a form is regarded as a tensor with its codimension-$r$ Tate twist. All homomorphisms and polarizations may be rational: an isomorphism in the isogeny category induces the usual isomorphisms on cohomology, including its tensor constructions.

We say that filtered isocrystal data have *finite local-field descent* if they are obtained by scalar extension from an isocrystal with its tensors over the maximal unramified subfield $H_0$ of a finite extension $H/\mathbb Q_p$, together with a filtration over $H$. The scalar extension is to $K_0H$. This condition will let us apply weak admissibility over a finite local field. For a group in a faithful representation, a tensor-preserving identification means one respecting a finite family of rational tensors whose stabilizer is that group, together with their comparison realizations.

<a id="stmt-prop-transport"></a>
<div id="prop:transport" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6** (Transport). *Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of positive dimension with good reduction at $w$, and put $V=H_1(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q)$. Suppose $$G\subset\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\psi)$$ is connected reductive, its Hodge homomorphism $h$ gives the Hodge structure of $A$, and its conjugacy class $X$ makes this inclusion a Hodge embedding. Let $\gamma$ be a rational Hodge class of codimension $r$, represented on $V$ by an alternating form satisfying <a href="#eq:transport-multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-multiplier">[eq:transport-multiplier]</a>.*

<a id="eq:transport-class"></a>
*Then there exist a positive integer $n$, an auxiliary good-reduction abelian variety $B$, a quasi-isogeny $a:A_0^n\to B_0$, and a rational Hodge class $\kappa$ of codimension $r$ on $B$ such that, for the first-factor inclusion $j:A_0\to A_0^n$, $$(aj)^*\kappa_0=\gamma_0.
 \label{eq:transport-class}$$ The equality holds simultaneously in every $\ell$-adic realization with $\ell\ne p$ and in the crystalline realization after extension to $C_w$. Either of the following two further specifications can be imposed.*

1.  *The variety $B$ is of CM type and is defined over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, with its indicated good reduction at $w$.*

<a id="eq:transport-prescribed-filtration"></a>
2.  *Put $D_A=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p]$. Suppose $\mathcal F_A$ is a filtration in degrees $0,1$ on $D_A$, over a finite extension of $K_0$, which is weakly admissible and has finite local-field descent together with the specialized tensors specifying the $G$-structure. Suppose also that, after a geometric tensor-preserving identification, it lies in the $G$-orbit of the original Hodge filtration. Then $B$ can be defined over a finite extension $K/K_0$ inside $C_w$, and $a$ can be chosen so that $$a^*\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(B/K)
           =(\mathcal F_A^1)^{\oplus n}.
     \label{eq:transport-prescribed-filtration}$$ Here the equality is made after a common finite extension and crystalline–de Rham identification. To discuss Hodge classes on this $B$, choose a complex embedding of an algebraic closure of $K$ extending the fixed embedding of $\overline{\mathbb Q}$.*

*In the second case the realizations of $\kappa$ are supplied by the canonical tensor sections of the Hodge-type model and the polarization. In the first case these agree with the usual realizations and specialization of its absolute Hodge class.*

</div>

We prepare two ingredients for the proof. First, we enlarge the given Hodge datum so that integral-model theorems apply at every prime while its rational representation remains a sum of copies of $V$. Second, we realize a prescribed admissible filtration by choosing a framed local Shimura point above its crystalline period and applying integral uniformization. The resulting abelian lift retains that point’s integral tensors and special-fiber marking. After these preparations, the proof constructs the transported tensor and normalizes it using rational divisor intersections.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### An auxiliary strongly admissible datum

We recall the hypotheses on integral models that we will use. For a reductive group $H_p/\mathbb Q_p$, a full Bruhat–Tits stabilizer is the smooth group scheme fixing a point of its extended building. Its identity component is the associated parahoric group scheme. Now let $(H,X_H)$ be a Shimura datum over $\mathbb Q$. In the terminology of <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Definition 2.1.6], the triple $(H,X_H,\mathcal H)$ is *strongly admissible* if $(H,X_H)$ is of Hodge type, $\mathcal H$ is a full stabilizer of $H_{\mathbb Q_p}$ with connected special fiber, the centralizer of a maximal $K_0$-split torus of $H_{K_0}$ is $R$-smooth, and the relative root system over $K_0$ is reduced when $p=2$. Here $R$-smoothness is the condition on tori used in <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[KisinZhouEarlier](#ref-KisinZhouEarlier), 2.4]. We will use it only for products and extensions of restrictions of scalars of split tori, for which it holds by <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[KisinZhouEarlier](#ref-KisinZhouEarlier), Proposition 2.4.6].

<a id="stmt-lem-auxiliary-datum"></a>
<div id="lem:auxiliary-datum" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Auxiliary Hodge datum). *Let $$(G,X)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow
 (\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\psi),X_{\mathrm{Siegel}})$$ be a Hodge embedding, with $G$ connected and similitude character $c$. For every prime $p$ there exist a totally real field $P$, a connected reductive group $J$, and a Hodge embedding $$(J,X_J)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow
 (\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(W,\psi_W),X'_{\mathrm{Siegel}})$$ with the following properties.*

1.  *There are inclusions of Shimura data induced by $$G\longrightarrow J\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P/\mathbb Q}G_P,$$ where the first map is diagonal and $J$ is the subgroup on which the similitude multipliers in the restriction of scalars agree.*

2.  *The group $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$ is quasi-split and has a full connected stabilizer $\mathcal J$ making $(J,X_J,\mathcal J)$ strongly admissible.*

<a id="eq:transport-copies"></a>
3.  *As a rational $J$-representation, $$W\simeq (V\otimes_{\mathbb Q}P)^{\oplus m}
     \label{eq:transport-copies}$$ for some positive integer $m$. The form $\psi_W$ has multiplier the common character $c$, and $\mathcal J\to\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(W_{\mathbb Q_p})$ extends to a closed immersion into $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(\Lambda_p)$ for a self-dual $\mathbb Z_p$-lattice $\Lambda_p$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-auxiliary-datum"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-auxiliary-datum</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_p$ splitting $G_{\mathbb Q_p}$. Polynomial approximation, together with Krasner’s lemma <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[MilneANT](#ref-MilneANT), Propositions 7.60–7.61 and Corollary 7.62], gives a totally real number field $P$ whose completions at $p$ split $G_{\mathbb Q_p}$. For example, approximate the minimal polynomial of a primitive element of a splitting extension at $p$, and a polynomial with distinct real roots at the real place. The resulting number field can have just one place above $p$; this additional condition is not needed.

<a id="eq:auxiliary-derived"></a>
Define the multiplier fiber product <a id="eq:equal-multiplier-group"></a>$$J=(\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P/\mathbb Q}G_P)
       \mathbin{\times}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P/\mathbb Q}\mathbf G_m}\mathbf G_m,
 \label{eq:equal-multiplier-group}$$ where the last map is diagonal. The Hodge cocharacter, or its inverse according to convention, pairs to $1$ with $c$. Since $c$ factors through $G^{\mathrm{ab}}$, its character on this torus is primitive. Thus $\ker(G^{\mathrm{ab}}\to\mathbf G_m)$ is a torus, and $\ker(c:G\to\mathbf G_m)$ is connected reductive. The exact sequence $$1\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P/\mathbb Q}(\ker c)_P
 \longrightarrow J\xrightarrow{c}\mathbf G_m\longrightarrow1$$ proves connectedness and reductivity of $J$. Moreover, $$J^{\mathrm{der}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P/\mathbb Q}G_P^{\mathrm{der}}.
 \label{eq:auxiliary-derived}$$

The action on $U=V\otimes_\mathbb QP$, with alternating form $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{P/\mathbb Q}\psi_P$, gives a symplectic embedding of $J$ with similitude character $c$. The diagonal Hodge homomorphism gives $X_J$. The adjoint Hodge-type and Cartan-involution axioms follow componentwise from those for $X$. We also check that extending the totally real field introduces no rational adjoint factor on which the Hodge homomorphism is trivial. Write a rational simple adjoint factor as $\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{L/\mathbb Q}H$, with $L$ totally real. Every field factor $L'$ of $P\otimes_\mathbb QL$ is totally real, and every real embedding of $L$ extends to a real embedding of $L'$. Hence the existence of a nontrivial Hodge component for $H$ persists in each resulting rational factor. Finally the diagonal weight is rational and the trace form is a polarization. This proves that $(J,X_J)$ is a Shimura datum of Hodge type.

<a id="eq:auxiliary-torus"></a>
<a id="eq:auxiliary-abelianization"></a>
Here are the local conditions explicitly. Put $P\otimes\mathbb Q_p=
\prod_{v\mid p}P_v$ and choose a split maximal torus $T_v$ of $G_{P_v}$. The character $c$ on $T_v$ has a cocharacter section: a conjugate of the Hodge cocharacter pairs to $1$ with it, and all cocharacters of a split torus are defined over $P_v$. The same argument applies to $G_{P_v}^{\mathrm{ab}}$. Consequently the maximal torus of $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$ obtained from the $T_v$, and the abelianization of $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$, have the forms $$\begin{aligned}
 T_J&\simeq\mathbf G_m\times
       \prod_{v\mid p}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P_v/\mathbb Q_p}\mathbf G_m^{\operatorname{rk}G-1},
       \label{eq:auxiliary-torus}\\
 J_{\mathbb Q_p}^{\mathrm{ab}}&\simeq\mathbf G_m\times
       \prod_{v\mid p}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{P_v/\mathbb Q_p}
                    \mathbf G_m^{\dim G^{\mathrm{ab}}-1}.
       \label{eq:auxiliary-abelianization}
\end{aligned}$$ The split Borels over the $P_v$ give a Borel of $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$, so this group is quasi-split. Its derived group is a product of restrictions of split groups by <a href="#eq:auxiliary-derived" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:auxiliary-derived">[eq:auxiliary-derived]</a>. The vertices from the hyperspecial vertices in those split groups give a very special parahoric of $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$. The inertia coinvariants of the cocharacter module in <a href="#eq:auxiliary-abelianization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:auxiliary-abelianization">[eq:auxiliary-abelianization]</a> are torsion-free, since that module is a direct sum of permutation modules. The full stabilizer is therefore connected by <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[KisinZhouEarlier](#ref-KisinZhouEarlier), Lemma 4.2.4].

The centralizer of a maximal $K_0$-split torus is obtained from <a href="#eq:auxiliary-torus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:auxiliary-torus">[eq:auxiliary-torus]</a> by unramified base extension; its torus factors are still induced split tori and $\mathbf G_m$. It is $R$-smooth by <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[KisinZhouEarlier](#ref-KisinZhouEarlier), Proposition 2.4.6]. Finally restrictions of split root systems give reduced relative root systems, also at $p=2$. These are precisely the strong-admissibility checks in the construction of <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Proposition 5.3.8]. They use the stipulated Hodge embedding, not the assertion that $G$ is the full Mumford–Tate group of a particular point.

Apply <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Proposition 2.1.9], starting with the representation $U$ just constructed. Its proof first tensors $U$ with an auxiliary totally real $\mathbb Q$-vector space, then takes direct sums, and finally applies the symplectic self-duality construction. The group acts trivially on that extra vector-space factor. In the last construction the dual representation twisted by $c$ is identified with $U$ by its symplectic form. Thus every resulting rational summand is a copy of $U$, with the same multiplier. This gives <a href="#eq:transport-copies" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-copies">[eq:transport-copies]</a>, together with $\psi_W$ and $\Lambda_p$. The assertion of the cited proposition includes $p=2$; its proof uses the reduced-relative-root condition through <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Lemma 2.1.8]. ◻

</details>

Choose a sufficiently small neat prime-to-$p$ level $K^p$ and put $K_p=\mathcal J(\mathbb Z_p)$. The adapted embedding gives the integral model $\mathcal S_K$ and its universal abelian scheme. A rational $\mathbb Z_{(p)}$-lattice can be chosen with completion $\Lambda_p$. We use a finite family of tensors defining its stabilizer $\mathcal J$, as in <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), 2.1.4]. Any additional rational $J$-invariant tensors may be included in this family after clearing denominators. Indeed their integral stabilizer condition already holds on the generic fiber; flatness of $\mathcal J$ makes it hold on $\mathcal J$ itself. Adding them therefore changes neither the group scheme nor its tensor trivializations.

The added tensors have compatible sections in every realization, as follows. Use the homological degree-one realizations in this paragraph, that is, the duals of the corresponding $H^1$, so that their reference representation is $W$. For such a realization $H_\nu$ over its coefficient field $k_\nu$, let $$\mathcal P_\nu=\operatorname{Isom}_{\{s_\alpha\}}
                    (W\otimes k_\nu,H_\nu)$$ be its torsor of frames preserving the original defining tensors. For a rational $J$-invariant tensor $u$ and a frame $e$, set $u_\nu=e(u)$. Two frames differ by an element of $J$, so this definition is independent of the frame and descends from a trivialization of the torsor. It also works integrally after clearing the fixed denominator of $u$.

The Betti and étale frame torsors are supplied by the canonical tensor sections of <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), 2.1.4]. The construction of the universal Breuil–Kisin–Fargues module and its structure tensors gives the crystalline torsor and its compatibility with those sections <a id="pm-cite-54"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), 2.1.13–2.1.14]. Indeed, a comparison map carrying every $s_\alpha$ to its specified realization carries a frame $e$ to the comparison of $e$; it therefore carries $e(u)$ to the same tensor evaluated in the new frame. Thus it preserves $u_\nu$ for every added invariant $u$, without requiring $u$ to be an expression in the chosen defining tensors. At a lift over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, these are the realizations of its rational Hodge tensor, by Lemma <a href="#lem:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:comparison">2</a>. Their crystalline specializations are independent of the lift because they are evaluations in the fixed crystalline frame torsor.

The same observation applies to isogenies. One quasi-isogeny preserving the defining tensors identifies their frame torsors in every realization in which those tensors are preserved. It therefore preserves all the added invariant tensors as well. This proves the tensor comparison and simultaneous preservation that will be used below; flatness alone is used only to keep the integral stabilizer unchanged.

Specification <span class="upright">(1)</span> of Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a> uses the special-point theorem <a id="pm-cite-55"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Theorem 2.2.7] directly. The next lemma is needed only for specification <span class="upright">(2)</span>, where the filtration has been prescribed in advance.

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
#### Realizing a prescribed admissible filtration

For a point $x\in\mathcal S_K(\mathbb F)$ let $C_x$ be the corresponding abelian variety and put $$\mathbb D_x=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(C_x/W(\mathbb F)),\qquad
 D_x=\mathbb D_x[1/p].$$ The crystalline structure tensors are denoted $s_{\alpha,0,x}$; the tensors away from $p$ are denoted $s_{\alpha,\ell,x}$. They are the specializations of the canonical tensor sections and are independent of the chosen lift of $x$.

The *tensor isogeny class* $\mathcal I_x$ consists of the points $y\in\mathcal S_K(\mathbb F)$ for which a single quasi-isogeny $C_x\to C_y$ preserves $s_{\alpha,0}$ and every $s_{\alpha,\ell}$ for $\ell\ne p$. We emphasize that this definition concerns actual quasi-isogenies of abelian varieties. It is the class defined in <a id="pm-cite-56"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), 2.2.4].

<a id="stmt-lem-admissible-realization"></a>
<div id="lem:admissible-realization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Realization of an admissible filtration). *Let $(J,X_J,\mathcal J)$ be strongly admissible, with $J_{\mathbb Q_p}$ quasi-split, and choose the adapted embedding and neat level above. Let $x\in\mathcal S_K(\mathbb F)$ have a lift to characteristic zero. Suppose a filtration $\mathcal F$ on $D_x$, after a finite extension of $K_0$, has the following properties:*

1.  *the resulting filtered isocrystal is admissible;*

2.  *geometrically its filtration is induced by a $J$-valued cocharacter in the conjugacy class prescribed by $X_J$.*

*Then, after extending that field finitely, there are $y\in\mathcal I_x$, a tensor-preserving quasi-isogeny $b:C_x\to C_y$, and a lift $\widetilde y$ over the extended valuation ring such that $$b^*\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1 H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(C_{\widetilde y})=\mathcal F^1$$ under the crystalline–de Rham identification.*

*In particular, condition <span class="upright">(1)</span> follows if the filtered isocrystal and its tensor data have finite local-field descent and $\mathcal F$ is weakly admissible.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-admissible-realization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-admissible-realization</summary>

*Proof.* Write $k=\mathbb F$ and let $\breve E$ be the completed local reflex field. We work over a common finite extension $K$ of $K_0$ containing $\breve E$ and a field of definition of $\mathcal F$. Every further field extension below is finite. Put $K_p=\mathcal J(\mathbb Z_p)$.

We first fix the relation between the group and cohomological conventions. Let $\rho:J_{\mathbb Q_p}\to\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(W_{\mathbb Q_p})$ be the adapted homological representation and let $\rho^\vee$ be its dual. Choose the tensor-preserving identification $\mathbb D_x\simeq\Lambda_p^\vee\otimes_{\mathbb Z_p}W(k)$ of <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), 2.1.14]. The local shtuka parameter is $b_x\in J(K_0)$, whereas crystalline Frobenius on this cohomological Dieudonné module is $$F_x=\rho^\vee(\sigma(b_x))\circ\sigma.$$ The additional $\sigma$ on $b_x$ comes from the comparison with the vector shtuka: its special-fiber module is $(\sigma^{-1})^*(\mathbb D_x^\vee)$ <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), Example 2.3.4 and Remark 2.3.10]. Write $\{\mu\}$ for the class prescribed by $X_J$; then $[b_x]\in B(J,\mu^{-1})$.

For precision, identify the general linear crystalline period flag with the appropriate filtration variety on $D_x$ by its marked Rapoport–Zink interpretation. If a deformation $\mathcal H$ has special-fiber marking $q:\mathcal H_k\dashrightarrow C_x[p^\infty]$, this identification sends its period to $(q^*)^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal H)$. Pushout through the faithful Hodge representation gives the commutative period square $$\begin{array}{ccc}
M_{\mathcal J,b_x,\mu,K_p}&\longrightarrow&M_{\mathop{\mathrm{GL}},\rho(b_x),\rho(\mu)}\\
\pi_{\mathrm{GM}}\downarrow&&\downarrow\pi_{\mathrm{GM}}\\
\mathcal F_{J,\mu}&\longrightarrow&\operatorname{Flag}(D_x).
\end{array}$$ The lower map is a closed immersion: the parabolic stabilizing the faithful filtration intersects $J$ in the parabolic for $\mu$. Define $f_{\mathcal F}$ to be the unique $J$-flag with image $\mathcal F$. It exists by condition <span class="upright">(2)</span>. The square uses crystalline periods and the dual Frobenius descent above, so no extension of $\sigma$ to the ramified filtration field is chosen. When $q=b^{-1}$ its lower right period is exactly $b^*\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1$.

*A framed point over the prescribed period.* Admissibility of the faithful filtered isocrystal implies that the image of $f_{\mathcal F}$ in the general linear period flag variety is in its admissible locus. To see this in the cohomological coordinates of the square, let $U$ be a crystalline representation realizing $(D_x,\mathcal F)$. Its crystalline comparison identifies the associated vector bundle away from the untilt with $U\otimes\mathcal O$. The filtered de Rham comparison identifies the modification at the untilt with the standard lattice $U\otimes B_{\mathrm{dR}}^+$. They glue to the trivial bundle $U\otimes\mathcal O$ on the Fargues–Fontaine curve, which is the general linear admissibility condition. Since $\rho$ is faithful, $\rho^{-1}(Z_{\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(W)})\subset Z_J$: an element acting as a scalar commutes with the faithful image of $J$. The admissible-locus identity in <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), Proposition 3.1.1(i), (3.1.9)] therefore puts $f_{\mathcal F}$ in $\mathcal F_{J,\mu}^{\mathrm{adm}}$.

<a id="eq:transport-framed-point"></a>
This locus is the image of the crystalline period map $$\pi_{\mathrm{GM}}:
 M_{\mathcal J,b_x,\mu,K_p}
       \longrightarrow\mathcal F_{J,\mu}^{\mathrm{adm}}.$$ The map is étale <a id="pm-cite-60"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), 3.1.1, (3.1.4)]. Its fiber at the classical point $f_{\mathcal F}$ is nonempty; an étale rigid space over $K$ has a point over a finite extension of $K$. After making that extension, choose $$z\in M_{\mathcal J,b_x,\mu,K_p}(K),
 \qquad \pi_{\mathrm{GM}}(z)=f_{\mathcal F}.
 \label{eq:transport-framed-point}$$ In particular $z$ supplies the integral level as well as the rational isocrystal marking. If $C$ is the completed algebraic closure of $K$, its moduli description gives an actual $\mathcal J$-torsor $\mathcal P_z$, a Frobenius isomorphism bounded by $\mu$, and, for sufficiently large $a$, an isomorphism $$i_a:\mathcal J_{Y_{[a,\infty)}(C^\flat)}
      \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
      \mathcal P_z|_{Y_{[a,\infty)}(C^\flat)},
 \qquad
 \varphi_{\mathcal P_z}\circ\varphi^*i_a
       =i_a\circ\varphi_{b_x}.$$ These are precisely the torsor and annular framing in <a id="pm-cite-61"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), 3.1.1]. Its associated pro-étale $\mathcal J(\mathbb Z_p)$-torsor gives integral tensor frames. Thus the integral structure and Frobenius-compatible framing are part of the chosen point, with all their tensor compatibilities.

*Integral realization and its special fiber.* Our adapted datum is of global Hodge type: the group scheme is a full connected Bruhat–Tits stabilizer, and the chosen Hodge embedding is integral on $\Lambda_p$. The center also satisfies the split-rank hypothesis of <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), 4.1]. Indeed its real subgroup of multiplier one preserves the positive definite form $\psi_W(v,h(i)w)$ and is compact. Its real split rank is therefore at most one, while the rational weight torus supplies one split central direction over $\mathbb Q$; both split ranks are one. The model here is the one in <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), 2.1.11 and Theorem 2.1.12], obtained from this adapted Hodge embedding and satisfying the Pappas–Rapoport characterization. Condition $(U_x)$ holds by <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[GleasonLimXu2026](#ref-GleasonLimXu2026), Corollary 6.3]; see also <a id="pm-cite-65"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), Remark 4.10.4]. Consequently <a id="pm-cite-66"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), Theorem 4.10.6] represents the integral local Shimura variety by a normal formal scheme $\mathfrak M_{\mathcal J,b_x,\mu}$ and gives the morphism $$\Theta_x:\mathfrak M_{\mathcal J,b_x,\mu}
          \longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal S_K}.$$ More precisely, <a id="pm-cite-67"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), 4.10.2, (4.10.2)–(4.10.6)] identifies this formal scheme with the tensor-compatible open and closed part of the pullback of the Siegel Rapoport–Zink space. This construction identifies the pullback of the universal $\mathcal J$-shtuka with its tautological framed shtuka and the faithful vector realization with the universal $p$-divisible group.

Its rigid generic fiber is the local Shimura variety <a id="pm-cite-68"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), Proposition 3.5.1(i)]. The classical point $z$ of this rigid generic fiber gives $\operatorname{Spf}\mathcal O_K\to\mathfrak M_{\mathcal J,b_x,\mu}$. Indeed it lies in the generic fiber of a formal affine chart; for a special formal chart one may take an affinoid member of its standard generic-fiber covering containing $z$. Evaluation of the formal chart’s ring is integral, and its ideal of definition maps to topologically nilpotent elements of $\mathcal O_K$. Let $z_0$ be the special point and put $y=\Theta_x(z_0)$ and $\widetilde y=\Theta_x(z)$. The formal map $\widetilde y$ gives a point of the integral model over $\mathcal O_K$ by restricting to an affine neighborhood of $y$. Pullback of the universal abelian scheme gives $C_{\widetilde y}$ and its $p$-divisible group $\mathcal H_z$.

We record explicitly the integral comparison at this point. For a uniformizer $\pi$ of $K$, put $\mathfrak S=W(k)[[u]]$, with $u\mapsto\pi$, and let $E_\pi(u)$ generate its kernel. The crystalline representation and integral level of $z$ give the $\mathcal J$-Breuil–Kisin torsor of <a id="pm-cite-69"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), 3.5], with $$\Phi:\varphi^*\mathcal P_{\mathrm{BK}}[1/E_\pi]
             \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
             \mathcal P_{\mathrm{BK}}[1/E_\pi].$$ Its pushout to $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(\Lambda_p)$ is the Breuil–Kisin module of $\mathcal H_z$. Reduction at $u=0$, followed by the dual Frobenius descent fixed above, therefore gives the actual Dieudonné lattice of $\mathcal H_{z,k}$. Proposition 3.5.1 of <a id="pm-cite-70"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024)] compares the two specializations of this already constructed framed point. In the coordinates of <a id="pm-cite-71"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Theorem 2.2.5] it gives $$g\in X_{\mathcal J}(\mu^{-1},b_x),\qquad
 \mathbb D(\mathcal H_{z,k})
       =\rho^\vee(\sigma(g))\mathbb D_x\subset D_x.$$ Indeed the Frobenius matrix on this lattice, written in its induced basis, is $$\rho^\vee\!\left(
       \sigma(g^{-1}b_x\sigma(g))\right)\circ\sigma,$$ which explains the same Frobenius descent in the Deligne–Lusztig condition and in the lattice formula.

*The abelian marking and the filtration.* Keep the identity prime-to-$p$ coordinate in the uniformization. The description of its special fiber in <a id="pm-cite-72"></a>[[PappasRapoport2024](#ref-PappasRapoport2024), 4.10.3], or equivalently the isogeny-class map of <a id="pm-cite-73"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Theorem 2.2.5], gives $y=i_x(g)$ and a single abelian quasi-isogeny $b:C_x\to C_y$ preserving the crystalline tensors and every prime-to-$p$ tensor. Its crystalline marking is the one just used for $\mathcal H_z$, because the uniformization is induced by the marked Siegel Rapoport–Zink space.

The crystalline period map of that space is the Hodge filtration of its universal deformation pulled back by its marking. Compatibility of $\Theta_x$ with the tautological shtuka and <a href="#eq:transport-framed-point" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-framed-point">[eq:transport-framed-point]</a> consequently give $$b^*\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(C_{\widetilde y}/K)
       =\mathcal F^1.$$ The deformation criterion <a id="pm-cite-74"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Proposition 2.2.3] is satisfied concretely: the filtration is of type $\mu$, and the crystalline structure tensors compare with the integral étale tensors in the $\mathcal J(\mathbb Z_p)$-frames of $z$. The bounded integral shtuka and its annular framing were obtained before using this criterion or comparing specializations. All cited global Hodge-type and strongly admissible results apply at $p=2$ as well.

Finally, under finite local-field descent, the weak-admissibility inequalities restrict to the descended Frobenius subobjects. Colmez–Fontaine’s theorem <a id="pm-cite-75"></a>[[ColmezFontaine2000](#ref-ColmezFontaine2000), Theorem A] makes those descended data admissible, and scalar extension supplies condition <span class="upright">(1)</span>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### Proof of the transport proposition

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-admissible-realization-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-admissible-realization</summary>

<a id="eq:transport-first-factor"></a>
*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a>.* *The original point and its reduction.* Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:auxiliary-datum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:auxiliary-datum">7</a>. Choose a rational $J$-equivariant identification $W=(V\otimes_\mathbb QP)^{\oplus m}$ and set $n=m[P:\mathbb Q]$. Choose a basis of $P$ whose first vector is $1$. The resulting identification of the Hodge structure at the diagonal point $h$ with $V^{\oplus n}$ makes the first-factor map equal to $$v\longmapsto(v\otimes1,0,\ldots,0).
 \label{eq:transport-first-factor}$$ The rational homology/isogeny correspondence <a id="pm-cite-76"></a>[[MilneShimura](#ref-MilneShimura), Corollary 6.9] therefore makes the abelian variety $C$ at that point isogenous to $A^n$. Fix a rational isogeny $f:A^n\to C$ realizing this identification.

We explain the field and reduction assertions for this starting point. The Hodge embedding maps it to the Siegel point of the polarized variety $C$ with finite level. The abelian variety $C$ is defined over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$: clearing a denominator in $f$ realizes it as a quotient of $A^n$ by a finite subgroup of its torsion, and every such subgroup is defined over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$. Its rational polarization and its finite level also descend to $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, since homomorphisms between abelian varieties over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ do not enlarge after extending algebraically closed fields <a id="pm-cite-77"></a>[[MilneAV](#ref-MilneAV), Corollary 20.4]. The Hodge embedding induces a finite morphism onto its image at sufficiently small finite level <a id="pm-cite-78"></a>[[MilneShimura](#ref-MilneShimura), Theorem 5.16]. Its fiber over a $\overline{\mathbb Q}$-point therefore consists of $\overline{\mathbb Q}$-points. This places the diagonal point on the canonical model over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$.

After a finite number-field extension the variety $C$ has good reduction, since it is isogenous to $A^n$ <a id="pm-cite-79"></a>[[MilneAVNotes](#ref-MilneAVNotes), Chapter IV, Theorem 3.5]. Its polarization and prime-to-$p$ level extend after a further finite extension. The integral Hodge-type model is the normalization of the closure of the generic fiber in the adapted Siegel model. The valuative criterion for this finite normalization therefore extends our point to its integral model. Denote the reduction by $x$. The rational isogeny $f$ specializes to a quasi-isogeny $f_0:A_0^n\to C_x$.

<a id="eq:transport-trace-restriction"></a>
*The trace tensor and its descent.* Next extend the alternating form defining $\gamma$ $P$-multilinearly and take its trace. Composing with projection onto the first copy in <a href="#eq:transport-copies" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-copies">[eq:transport-copies]</a> gives <a id="eq:transport-trace-tensor"></a>$$\eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{P/\mathbb Q}(\gamma_P)\circ
              (\operatorname{pr}_1)^{\otimes 2r}
       \in\bigwedge^{2r}W^*.
 \label{eq:transport-trace-tensor}$$ For $r=0$ this is interpreted as the scalar trace. The equal-multiplier condition implies that $\eta$ has multiplier $c^r$ under $J$. On <a href="#eq:transport-first-factor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-first-factor">[eq:transport-first-factor]</a>, its restriction is $$j^*f^*\eta_C=[P:\mathbb Q]\gamma.
 \label{eq:transport-trace-restriction}$$ The class $\eta_C$ is understood with its Tate twist.

<a id="eq:transport-untwisted-tensors"></a>
We turn this semi-invariant into the untwisted tensors to which the integral-model theorems directly apply. Write $\psi_W^{-1}\in\bigwedge^2W$ for the inverse symplectic bivector, and include $$u_\psi=\psi_W\otimes\psi_W^{-1},\qquad
 u_\eta=\eta\otimes(\psi_W^{-1})^{\otimes r}
 \label{eq:transport-untwisted-tensors}$$ among the structure tensors. Both are $J$-invariant tensors in ordinary tensor constructions of $W$ and $W^*$; no separately trivialized Tate object is needed. They can be made integral and added to the defining family as explained above. Their crystalline and prime-to-$p$ realizations at $x$ are the specializations from the original point, with the comparison conventions fixed above.

<span id="par:transport-tensor-descent" label="par:transport-tensor-descent"></span> The entire finite family of structure tensors, including $u_\psi$ and $u_\eta$, has finite local-field descent. To see this, choose a finite extension $L/\mathbb Q_p$ over whose valuation ring the original number-field point is defined, and let $L_0$ be its maximal unramified subfield. The canonical étale tensor sections of <a id="pm-cite-80"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), 2.1.4], pulled back to that point, are $\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\overline L/L)$-invariant. Applying the tensor functor $D_{\mathrm{cris},L}$ to these morphisms from the unit object places them in the crystalline tensor spaces over $L_0$. Their extension to $K_0$ is exactly the family $s_{\alpha,0,x}$, by good-reduction comparison. The map $f_0$ also descends after a finite residue-field extension, so this descent is retained under its marking. Taking a compositum with the finite field of definition of a prescribed filtration therefore gives one finite local field for all the data. No tensor-preserving trivialization over $L_0$ is being asserted or needed.

*The two choices of lift.* For specification (1), apply <a id="pm-cite-81"></a>[[KisinZhou2025](#ref-KisinZhou2025), Theorem 2.2.7] to $x$. It gives a point $y\in\mathcal I_x$ with a special-point lift and a tensor-preserving quasi-isogeny $b:C_x\to C_y$. The special-point lift supplies a CM abelian variety $B$. Special points on the canonical Shimura variety are algebraic over its reflex field by the canonical-model theory <a id="pm-cite-82"></a>[[MilneShimura](#ref-MilneShimura), Definition 12.8 and Proposition 14.14], so $B$ and its level data can be taken over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$. The given local lift specifies their embedding at $w$ and their good reduction $B_0=C_y$.

For specification (2), identify $D_x$ with $D_A^{\oplus n}$ using $f_0^*$ and prescribe the direct-sum filtration $\mathcal F_A^{\oplus n}$. It is weakly admissible, with the required finite local-field descent. It is also in the required $J$-cocharacter orbit. Indeed a geometric element of the transported $G$ taking the original filtration on $D_A$ to $\mathcal F_A$ acts diagonally on all the copies of $V$ in $W$; its image in $J$ takes the original filtration on $D_x$ to the prescribed one. The tensor-preserving identifications used here come from the initial point and comparison, so they include the $J$-structure tensors as well. Geometric conjugacy can be realized over a finite extension, since the corresponding isomorphism scheme is of finite type. Lemma <a href="#lem:admissible-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:admissible-realization">8</a> now gives $y$, its lift $B$, and a tensor-preserving quasi-isogeny $b:C_x\to B_0$ with the prescribed marked filtration. This proves <a href="#eq:transport-prescribed-filtration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-prescribed-filtration">[eq:transport-prescribed-filtration]</a> for $a=bf_0$.

<a id="eq:transport-local-similitudes"></a>
<a id="eq:transport-rational-similitude"></a>
*A common rational normalization.* It remains, in both specifications, to recover the twisted class and normalize it by a single rational scalar. Choose a complex description of the lifted Shimura point. Its rational marking by $W$ supplies a rational Hodge class $\eta_B$ corresponding to $\eta$, and a rational divisor class $\theta_B$ corresponding to $\psi_W$. Write $\theta_C$ for the analogous class on $C$ and put $$\theta_*=f_0^*\theta_{C,0}\in\mathop{\mathrm{NS}}(A_0^n)\otimes_\mathbb Z\mathbb Q.$$ The class $\theta_*$ is rational ample. Tensor preservation of $u_\psi$ says that, in each realization, $$a^*\theta_{B,0}=\lambda_v\theta_*,
 \label{eq:transport-local-similitudes}$$ with a nonzero local scalar $\lambda_v$. Let $D=n\dim A$. Pairing with $\theta_*^{D-1}$ gives $$\lambda_v=
 \frac{\deg\bigl(a^*\theta_{B,0}\cdot\theta_*^{D-1}\bigr)}
      {\deg(\theta_*^D)}=:\lambda\in\mathbb Q^\times.
 \label{eq:transport-rational-similitude}$$ Both intersection numbers refer to rational divisor classes on the same abelian variety; their cycle-class traces are identical in every realization. The denominator is positive because $\theta_*$ is ample, and the numerator is nonzero by <a href="#eq:transport-local-similitudes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-local-similitudes">[eq:transport-local-similitudes]</a>. Thus all the scalars in <a href="#eq:transport-local-similitudes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-local-similitudes">[eq:transport-local-similitudes]</a>, including the crystalline one, are the single rational number $\lambda$.

<a id="eq:transport-eta-scaling"></a>
Preservation of $u_\eta$, together with <a href="#eq:transport-rational-similitude" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-rational-similitude">[eq:transport-rational-similitude]</a>, then yields $$a^*\eta_{B,0}=\lambda^r f_0^*\eta_{C,0}
 \label{eq:transport-eta-scaling}$$ simultaneously. In fact the inverse bivector scales by $\lambda^{-1}$, so that the equality for the untwisted tensor in <a href="#eq:transport-untwisted-tensors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-untwisted-tensors">[eq:transport-untwisted-tensors]</a> gives exactly <a href="#eq:transport-eta-scaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-eta-scaling">[eq:transport-eta-scaling]</a>. Set $$\kappa=\frac{\lambda^{-r}}{[P:\mathbb Q]}\eta_B.$$ Specializing <a href="#eq:transport-trace-restriction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-trace-restriction">[eq:transport-trace-restriction]</a> and using <a href="#eq:transport-eta-scaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-eta-scaling">[eq:transport-eta-scaling]</a> proves <a href="#eq:transport-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transport-class">[eq:transport-class]</a>.

For the arbitrary local lift, the crystalline realization just used is unambiguous: the canonical model supplies the untwisted tensor $u_\eta$, and the algebraic polarization supplies its Tate line and inverse bivector. The comparison isomorphism and these operations define $\eta_B$ in the crystalline realization and agree with its canonical étale tensors. Consequently any identity expressing this class by algebraic divisor operations specializes with the same rational coefficients in all realizations. For the $\overline{\mathbb Q}$-valued CM lift, these are also the usual absolute-Hodge and de Rham realizations. This completes the two constructions. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-cm-transport"></a>
<div id="cor:cm-transport" class="corollary">

**Corollary 9** (CM transport of a Hodge class). *Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of positive dimension with good reduction at $w$, and let $\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$ be a rational Hodge class, where $0\leq r\leq\dim A$. There are a positive integer $n$, a CM abelian variety $B/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$, a rational Hodge class $\kappa$ of codimension $r$ on $B$, and a quasi-isogeny $a:A_0^n\to B_0$ such that $$\gamma_0=(aj)^*\kappa_0,$$ where $j:A_0\to A_0^n$ is the first-factor inclusion. This is an equality of the usual absolute-Hodge specializations in every prime-to-$p$ realization and in crystalline cohomology extended to $C_w$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-cm-transport"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-cm-transport</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a polarization $\psi$ on $V=H_1(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q)$, and let $G=\mathop{\mathrm{MT}}(V)$. The Mumford–Tate group is connected reductive and is characterized by its Hodge tensors <a id="pm-cite-83"></a>[[Deligne1982](#ref-Deligne1982), Section 3, Propositions 3.4 and 3.6]. The Hodge homomorphism and its $G(\mathbb R)$-conjugacy class define the Mumford–Tate Shimura datum of Hodge type, with faithful symplectic representation $$G\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\psi).$$ The polarization supplies the rational weight and the Cartan involution condition. The definition of the Mumford–Tate group excludes an adjoint factor on which the Hodge homomorphism is trivial. The class $\gamma$, with its Tate twist, is fixed by $G$. Equivalently, its alternating form on $V$ has multiplier $c^r$, where $c$ is the similitude character. Thus the first alternative of Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a> applies and gives the asserted data and simultaneous equality. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:filtration"></a>
### An endomorphism-stable weakly admissible filtration

We construct a filtration on first crystalline cohomology that preserves an additional divisor. The construction takes place entirely on the given isocrystal. Its realization by an abelian lift will be a separate step. Generic transversality is a basic ingredient of the admissible-filtration existence theorems of Fontaine–Rapoport <a id="pm-cite-84"></a>[[FontaineRapoport2005](#ref-FontaineRapoport2005), Theorem 1 and Lemmas 1.1–1.2]. We impose the additional endomorphism and polarization constraints explicitly. The bounded-degree argument below chooses the required coordinates over a finite algebraic extension, while testing every subisocrystal over the fixed coefficient field.

Throughout this section, $K_0=\operatorname{Frac}W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)$, and valuations are normalized by $v(p)=1$. If $D$ is an isocrystal over $K_0$ and $K/K_0$ is finite, a subspace $L\subset D_K$ defines the one-step filtration $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^iD_K=
 \begin{cases}
 D_K,&i\leq 0,\\
 L,&i=1,\\
 0,&i\geq 2.
 \end{cases}$$ For an isocrystal subobject $P\subset D$, its Hodge number for this filtration is $t_H(P)=\dim_K(P_K\cap L)$. Its Newton number $t_N(P)$ is the sum of its slopes with multiplicities. Weak admissibility means that $t_H(D)=t_N(D)$ and $t_H(P)\leq t_N(P)$ for every isocrystal subobject $P$ over $K_0$.

Finite local-field descent is understood as in Section <a href="#sec:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transport">3</a>: the isocrystal and its indicated tensors descend to the maximal unramified subfield of a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_p$, and the filtration descends to that finite extension.

<a id="stmt-thm-filtration"></a>
<div id="thm:filtration" class="theorem">

**Theorem 10** (The filtration construction). *Let $A_0/\overline{\mathbb F}_p$ be an abelian variety equipped with a rational action of a CM field $E$ and a polarization whose Rosati involution restricts to conjugation on $E$. Suppose that $$D=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p))[1/p]$$ has rank $2s$ over $E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0$, where $s\geq 1$. Suppose also that there is an invertible $u\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A_0)$ such that $$u^\dagger=u,\qquad ue=\bar e u\quad(e\in E).$$ Let $\beta$ be the dual polarization pairing on $D$, with values in the isocrystal line of slope $1$.*

*Then there are a finite extension $K/K_0$ and a subspace $L\subset
D_K$ with the following properties:*

1.  *The one-step filtration with $\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1D_K=L$ is weakly admissible.*

2.  *$L$ is stable under the cohomological actions of $E$ and $u$, and is Lagrangian for $\beta$.*

3.  *After extending to an algebraically closed field $\Omega/K$, one has $\dim_\Omega L_\tau=s$ for every embedding $\tau:E\hookrightarrow\Omega$.*

4.  *The isocrystal, its indicated tensors, and this filtration have finite local field descent.*

*Moreover, geometrically $L$ belongs to the balanced polarized cocharacter orbit. More explicitly, define $$G_D=\left\{g\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(D,\beta)\cap
 \mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_{E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0}(D):
 \det_{E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0}(g)=c(g)^s\right\},$$ where the scalar on the right is viewed in $(E\otimes_\mathbb QK_0)^\times$. The balanced $E$-stable $\beta$-Lagrangians form a single $G_D(\Omega)$-orbit. Each is the weight-$1$ space of a cocharacter of $G_{D,\Omega}$ with weights $0,1$. In particular $L$ belongs to the orbit of any original filtration of this type under a tensor-preserving comparison.*

</div>

The proof will construct $L$ from Lagrangians in finitely many symplectic spaces. The two lemmas below supply the required simultaneous choice: minimum intersection with a subspace is ensured by a nonzero minor of bounded degree, and all such minors over a fixed coefficient field can be made nonzero over one finite extension.

<a id="stmt-lem-avoidance"></a>
<div id="lem:avoidance" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Bounded-degree avoidance over a finite extension). *Let $K_1/K_0$ be a finite extension, and let $r,b\geq 1$ be integers. There exist an element $\theta$ algebraic over $\mathbb Q_p$ and a point $$(x_0,\ldots,x_{r-1})\in K_1(\theta)^r$$ at which every nonzero polynomial in $K_1[X_0,\ldots,X_{r-1}]$ of total degree at most $b$ is nonzero. The coordinates $x_i$ can all be chosen in $\mathbb Q_p(\theta)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-avoidance"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-avoidance</summary>

*Proof.* Choose an integer $N>b$ and put $$B=b\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}N^i.$$ Let $e$ be the ramification index of $K_1/K_0$, so that $v(K_1^\times)=\frac1e\mathbb Z$. Choose a prime $q>\max(B,e)$, and choose $\theta$ with $\theta^q=p$ in an algebraic closure of $K_1$. The value $v(\theta)=1/q$ has order $q$ modulo $\frac1e\mathbb Z$. Consequently the ramification index, and hence the degree, of $K_1(\theta)/K_1$ is at least $q$. The polynomial $X^q-p$ gives the reverse inequality, so $$[K_1(\theta):K_1]=q>B.$$

Set $x_i=\theta^{N^i}$. Under the substitution $X_i=T^{N^i}$, distinct monomials of total degree at most $b$ have distinct exponents: each exponent of an individual variable is smaller than $N$, and uniqueness follows from base-$N$ expansion. Thus a nonzero polynomial of total degree at most $b$ becomes a nonzero polynomial in $T$ of degree at most $B$. It cannot vanish at $\theta$, whose degree over $K_1$ exceeds $B$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 12*. The coefficient field in Lemma <a href="#lem:avoidance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:avoidance">11</a> is the fixed field $K_1$. The conclusion concerns all bounded-degree polynomials over that field, with no restriction on their cardinality. It would be false with $K_1(\theta)$ in place of $K_1$ as the coefficient field.

</div>

<a id="stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection"></a>
<div id="lem:lagrangian-intersection" class="lemma">

**Lemma 13** (Intersections with Lagrangians). *Let $M$ be a symplectic space of dimension $2m$ over a field $k$ of characteristic zero, and let $W\subset M$ have dimension $a$. A nonempty open subset of the Lagrangian Grassmannian consists of subspaces $L$ such that $$\dim(L\cap W)=\max(0,a-m).$$ On any fixed symplectic affine big cell, at least one nonzero polynomial of degree at most $m$ has nonvanishing locus contained in this open subset. This degree bound is independent of $W$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection</summary>

*Proof.* We first work over an algebraic closure. The Lagrangian Grassmannian is geometrically integral of dimension $m(m+1)/2$; its affine big cells are parametrized by symmetric $m\times m$ matrices. If $1\leq a\leq m$, consider the projective incidence variety of pairs $(\ell,L)$ with $\ell\subset W$ a line and $\ell\subset L$ a Lagrangian. The fiber over $\ell$ is the Lagrangian Grassmannian of the symplectic space $\ell^\perp/\ell$, and therefore the incidence variety has dimension $$a-1+\frac{m(m-1)}2<\frac{m(m+1)}2.$$ Its image is a proper closed subset. Its complement consists precisely of the Lagrangians missing $W$. The case $a=0$ is immediate. For $a>m$, use $$\dim(L\cap W)=a-m+\dim(L\cap W^\perp)$$ and apply the preceding argument to $W^\perp$.

For the assertion on equations, choose coordinates on the fixed big cell in which $L$ is the column space of $$\begin{pmatrix}I_m\\ T\end{pmatrix},\qquad T=T^{\mathsf t}.$$ The minimum-intersection condition says that the composite of this matrix with the quotient map $M\longrightarrow M/W$ has rank $\min(m,2m-a)$. Its entries are affine linear polynomials in the independent entries of $T$. The nonempty open subset already proved meets this big cell, so some minor of the required size is a nonzero polynomial over $k$. Its degree is at most $m$, and its nonvanishing implies the required rank. If the required rank is zero, use the constant polynomial $1$. ◻

</details>

The two lemmas reduce simultaneous transversality to a uniform bound on the degrees of the relevant minors. We now identify the symplectic spaces on which to apply them and the inequalities they must enforce.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-lagrangian-intersection</summary>

<a id="eq:filtration-adjoints"></a>
*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a>.* We write $e$ and $u$ also for their cohomological actions on $D$. These actions commute with Frobenius and satisfy $$\label{eq:filtration-adjoints}
 \beta(ux,y)=\beta(x,uy),\qquad
 \beta(ex,y)=\beta(x,\bar e y),\qquad ue=\bar e u.$$ There is no change to the last relation from contravariance. Indeed, pulling back $ue=\bar e u$ gives $e^*u^*=u^*\bar e^*$; replacing $e$ by $\bar e$ gives $u^*e^*=\bar e^*u^*$. Likewise, if $J$ is the homological polarization matrix, $U^{\mathsf t}J=JU$ implies $UJ^{-1}=J^{-1}U^{\mathsf t}$, which is the self-adjointness of the cohomological action $U^{\mathsf t}$ for the dual pairing. The Rosati-conjugate adjoint identity for $E$ follows in the same way.

The pullback $U^{\mathsf t}$ of an individual endomorphism is distinct from the contragredient group action $g\mapsto g^{-\mathsf t}$. If a homological similitude $g$ has multiplier $c_V$ and satisfies $\det_E(g)=c_V^s$, its dual action has multiplier $c_D=c_V^{-1}$ and satisfies $\det_E(g^{-\mathsf t})=c_D^s$. Thus $G_D$ is the dual form of the homological group defined by this determinant condition, with its cohomological multiplier.

*Reduction to the stable subobjects.* First consider any $E,u$-stable candidate $L\subset D_K$, where $K/K_0$ is finite. We show that the weak-admissibility inequalities need only be tested on $E,u$-stable subobjects. This resembles the use of canonical destabilizing objects in <a id="pm-cite-85"></a>[[FontaineRapoport2005](#ref-FontaineRapoport2005), Section 2], but we use total defect, not defect divided by dimension, and give the required elementary argument. For any isocrystal subobject $P\subset D$ over $K_0$, set $$f(P)=t_H(P)-t_N(P).$$ These values form a finite set: the Hodge number is a bounded integer, and the Newton number is determined by bounded integer multiplicities among the finitely many slopes of $D$. Let $a$ be their maximum. Newton numbers are additive in short exact sequences, while $$(P_K\cap L)+(Q_K\cap L)\subset (P+Q)_K\cap L.$$ Consequently <a id="eq:filtration-supermodularity"></a>$$\label{eq:filtration-supermodularity}
 f(P)+f(Q)\leq f(P\cap Q)+f(P+Q).$$ If $P$ and $Q$ both attain $a$, the right-hand terms are each at most $a$, so both attain $a$. Thus maximizers are closed under intersection and sum. Choose a maximizer $P_{\max}$ of largest dimension. Adding any other maximizer to it shows that the other one is contained in it. Hence $P_{\max}$ is the unique largest maximizer.

The operators $u$ and $e\in E^\times$ are filtration-preserving isocrystal automorphisms. They preserve $f$ and hence fix $P_{\max}$, by its uniqueness. Thus $P_{\max}$ is $E,u$-stable. Consequently, if $t_H(D')\leq t_N(D')$ for every $E,u$-stable subobject $D'$, then $a=f(P_{\max})\leq0$. All the weak-admissibility inequalities follow. We must therefore construct an $E,u$-stable Lagrangian with these restricted inequalities and $t_H(D)=t_N(D)$.

*Semisimplicity and symplectic blocks.* The Rosati trace form $$\langle a,b\rangle=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(ab^\dagger)
 \quad\text{on }\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A_0)\otimes_\mathbb Q\mathbb R$$ is positive definite <a id="pm-cite-86"></a>[[MilneAV](#ref-MilneAV), Theorem 17.3]. Left multiplication by $u$ is self-adjoint for this form, since trace cyclicity gives $$\langle ua,b\rangle=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(uab^\dagger)
 =\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(ab^\dagger u)=\langle a,ub\rangle.$$ It is therefore diagonalizable. Its minimal polynomial equals the minimal polynomial of $u$ in the endomorphism algebra, as is seen by applying a polynomial in the multiplication operator to the identity. Thus $u$, and hence $u^2$, acts semisimply on $D$. The relation in <a href="#eq:filtration-adjoints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-adjoints">[eq:filtration-adjoints]</a> also shows that $u^2$ commutes with $E$.

Put $$B(x,y)=\beta(x,uy).$$ This is a nondegenerate alternating pairing. Indeed, invertibility is immediate, and $B(y,x)=-\beta(ux,y)=-B(x,y)$. In addition, $$B(ex,y)=\beta(x,\bar euy)=\beta(x,uey)=B(x,ey),
 \qquad B(u^2x,y)=B(x,u^2y).$$ After a finite splitting extension $K_1/K_0$, write $$D_{K_1}=\bigoplus_{\tau,z}M_{\tau,z},
 \qquad
 M_{\tau,z}=\{x:ex=\tau(e)x\ (e\in E),\ u^2x=zx\}.$$ We omit zero summands. The self-adjoint identities for $B$ show that distinct summands are orthogonal. Its restriction to each summand is therefore nondegenerate. Write $\dim M_{\tau,z}=2m_{\tau,z}$.

<a id="eq:filtration-u-similitude"></a>
<a id="eq:filtration-opposite-choice"></a>
For $\bar\tau=\tau\circ\overline{(\cdot)}$, the operator $u$ maps $M_{\tau,z}$ isomorphically to $M_{\bar\tau,z}$. On these spaces, $$\label{eq:filtration-u-similitude}
 B(ux,uy)=zB(x,y).$$ Here $z\neq 0$, and $\tau\neq\bar\tau$. Choose a $B$-Lagrangian $L_{\tau,z}$ in one member of each opposite pair, and prescribe $$\label{eq:filtration-opposite-choice}
 L_{\bar\tau,z}=uL_{\tau,z}.$$ Equation <a href="#eq:filtration-u-similitude" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-u-similitude">[eq:filtration-u-similitude]</a> shows that the latter is Lagrangian as well. Their direct sum $L$ is $E$-stable and $u$-stable; on applying $u$ twice, one obtains $zL_{\tau,z}=L_{\tau,z}$. Since $$\beta(x,y)=B(x,u^{-1}y),$$ $L$ is also $\beta$-Lagrangian. Its dimension at each $E$-embedding is $s$. It remains to make the independent choices appropriately.

*The inherited Newton grading.* Use the canonical slope decomposition $$D=\bigoplus_\lambda D_\lambda.$$ The isocrystal category over $\overline{\mathbb F}_p$ is semisimple, and this decomposition is functorial <a id="pm-cite-87"></a>[[ColmezFontaine2000](#ref-ColmezFontaine2000), 3.2, Proposition 3.3]. The slopes of $D$ belong to $[0,1]$: its integral Dieudonné operators $F,V$ satisfy $FV=p$, so both the slopes of $F$ and those of $V$ are nonnegative. All the endomorphisms above preserve every $D_\lambda$, and the projectors onto the simultaneous eigenspaces preserve its scalar extension. Thus each block has a grading $$M_{\tau,z}=\bigoplus_\lambda M_{\tau,z,\lambda},\qquad
 M_{\tau,z,\lambda}=M_{\tau,z}\cap(D_\lambda)_{K_1}.$$ We use this grading even if Frobenius permutes the labels $(\tau,z)$. No extension of Frobenius to $K_1$, and no isocrystal structure on an individual block, is required.

<a id="eq:filtration-block-newton"></a>
Before scalar extension, $B$ is a pairing of isocrystals into the slope-$1$ line. Hence $B(D_\lambda,D_\mu)=0$ unless $\lambda+\mu=1$. Nondegeneracy on an individual block $M$ then gives perfect pairings between $M_\lambda$ and $M_{1-\lambda}$. In particular, if $\dim M=2m$, $$\label{eq:filtration-block-newton}
 \sum_\lambda\lambda\dim M_\lambda=m.$$

<a id="eq:filtration-block-bound"></a>
Let $D'\subset D$ be an isocrystal subobject over $K_0$ stable under $E$ and $u$. Its intersections $M'=D'_{K_1}\cap M$ decompose $D'_{K_1}$ and inherit the same slope grading. Put $a=\dim M'$. The slope sum of the graded quotient $M/M'$ is at most $2m-a$, because all its slopes belong to $[0,1]$. By <a href="#eq:filtration-block-newton" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-block-newton">[eq:filtration-block-newton]</a>, $$\label{eq:filtration-block-bound}
 \sum_\lambda\lambda\dim M'_\lambda
 \geq \max(0,a-m).$$ The additional bound by zero follows from the nonnegative slopes. The right side is also the smallest possible dimension of the intersection of a Lagrangian in $M$ with $M'$. Thus attaining this minimum in every block will give the weak-admissibility inequalities for all the stable subobjects.

*Simultaneous choices and finite local field descent.* We may arrange the splitting field and coordinates used above as follows. Choose a finite field $k_0\subset\overline{\mathbb F}_p$ over which $A_0$, its polarization, $u$, and a rational basis of $E$ are defined. The underlying isocrystal and the indicated tensors then descend to $\operatorname{Frac}W(k_0)$. Choose a finite local field $H/\mathbb Q_p$ containing this field and splitting the actions of $E$ and $u^2$. The eigenspaces and their symplectic bases can be constructed over $H$ by linear algebra. We take $K_1=K_0H$. In particular, the independent Lagrangian Grassmannians, fixed symplectic big cells in them, and the maps induced by $u$ all have coordinates over $H$.

<a id="eq:filtration-minimal-intersection"></a>
Take the product of these big cells, one for each independent choice in <a href="#eq:filtration-opposite-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-opposite-choice">[eq:filtration-opposite-choice]</a>, and let $r$ be its number of affine coordinates. For every $E,u$-stable isocrystal subobject $D'$ over $K_0$ and each independent block $M$, the space $M'=D'_{K_1}\cap M$ is defined over the *fixed* field $K_1$. Lemma <a href="#lem:lagrangian-intersection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lagrangian-intersection">13</a> supplies a nonzero polynomial over $K_1$, of degree at most $m$, whose nonvanishing ensures $$\label{eq:filtration-minimal-intersection}
 \dim(L_{\tau,z}\cap M')=\max(0,\dim M'-m).$$ We regard this as a polynomial on the whole product by ignoring the other coordinates. A single bound $b=\max m_{\tau,z}$ works for all these polynomials, independently of $D'$. On the opposite block, the same intersection equality follows from $u$-stability of $D'$ and <a href="#eq:filtration-opposite-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-opposite-choice">[eq:filtration-opposite-choice]</a>.

Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:avoidance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:avoidance">11</a> to $K_1,r,b$. Its one point satisfies <a href="#eq:filtration-minimal-intersection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-minimal-intersection">[eq:filtration-minimal-intersection]</a> for every one of the subobjects just specified, simultaneously. This use of the lemma does not assume that the collection of subobjects is countable or that its members descend to $H$. Their definition over $K_1$ and the uniform polynomial degree bound are the only requirements. There are nonzero blocks with $m\geq1$, so $r,b\geq1$ as required.

Put $K=K_1(\theta)=K_0H(\theta)$. The resulting Lagrangian is defined over $H(\theta)$ in the chosen coordinates, and $H(\theta)$ is finite over $\mathbb Q_p$. Extending the original isocrystal to the maximal unramified subfield of $H(\theta)$ therefore gives finite local field descent of the complete filtered data. In particular, the construction has not obtained its generic point by introducing transcendental parameters.

Summing <a href="#eq:filtration-minimal-intersection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-minimal-intersection">[eq:filtration-minimal-intersection]</a> and <a href="#eq:filtration-block-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-block-bound">[eq:filtration-block-bound]</a> over all blocks gives <a id="eq:filtration-stable-test"></a>$$\label{eq:filtration-stable-test}
 t_H(D')\leq t_N(D')
 \quad\text{for every $E,u$-stable isocrystal subobject $D'\subset D$.}$$ For $D$ itself, both numbers equal $\frac12\dim D$ by <a href="#eq:filtration-block-newton" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filtration-block-newton">[eq:filtration-block-newton]</a>. The reduction to stable subobjects proved at the start now gives all the weak-admissibility inequalities for subobjects of $D$ over $K_0$. Each subobject over the smaller unramified field of descent extends to one of these subobjects. Hence the descended filtered isocrystal is weakly admissible as well.

*The geometric cocharacter orbit.* Over $\Omega$, the polarization pairs $D_\tau$ only with $D_{\bar\tau}$. A balanced $E$-stable Lagrangian is determined by an $s$-dimensional subspace of $D_\tau$ for one member of each pair; the subspace in the opposite member is its annihilator. The subgroup of $G_D$ with multiplier $1$ contains the product of $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}(D_\tau)$ acting on opposite spaces by the inverse dual representations. These special linear groups are transitive on the $s$-dimensional subspaces. Thus all the indicated Lagrangians form one $G_D(\Omega)$-orbit.

Finally choose an $E$-stable $\beta$-Lagrangian complement to one such $L$. Such a complement is obtained separately on each pair of opposite embedding spaces. Acting by $t$ on $L$ and by $1$ on the complement gives a cocharacter with multiplier $t$ and $E$-determinant $t^s$ at each embedding. It lies in $G_D$ and has the stated filtration. This proves the final assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-filtration-admissible"></a>
<div id="cor:filtration-admissible" class="corollary">

**Corollary 14**. *The filtration of Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a>, with its finite local field descent, is admissible: the descended filtered isocrystal is the filtered crystalline module of a crystalline representation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-filtration-admissible"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-filtration-admissible</summary>

*Proof.* Apply the weakly-admissible-implies-admissible theorem of Colmez–Fontaine with monodromy operator zero <a id="pm-cite-88"></a>[[ColmezFontaine2000](#ref-ColmezFontaine2000), Theorem A]. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-filtration-divisor"></a>
<div id="cor:filtration-divisor" class="corollary">

**Corollary 15** (The divisor filtration condition). *Let $D,L$ be as in Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a>, and let $\psi$ and $U$ denote the homological polarization and the homological action of $u$ on $D_K^\vee$. The untwisted divisor form $\psi(\cdot,U\cdot)$ belongs to $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1\bigl(\bigwedge^2D_K\bigr)=L\wedge D_K,$$ and hence to filtration degree zero after its codimension-one Tate twist. If a marked power is realized by an abelian lift, the corresponding pulled-back divisor satisfies the filtration condition for its symmetric homomorphism in Lemma <a href="#lem:lift-hom" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lift-hom">5</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-filtration-divisor"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-filtration-divisor</summary>

*Proof.* Let $H\subset D_K^\vee$ be the annihilator of $L$. It is Lagrangian for $\psi$ and stable under $U$: for $a\in L$ and $h\in H$, $$a(Uh)=(u^*a)(h)=0.$$ Therefore $\psi(h,Uh')=0$ for $h,h'\in H$. The divisor form thus lies in $$\ker\bigl(\bigwedge^2D_K\longrightarrow\bigwedge^2H^\vee\bigr)
       =L\wedge D_K.$$ This proves the assertion on the fixed filtered vector space. After realization of a marked power, pullback respects this filtration condition, which is precisely the condition on the associated symmetric homomorphism in Lemma <a href="#lem:lift-hom" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lift-hom">5</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:weil"></a>
### Balanced Weil classes

Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ have good reduction at the fixed place $w$, and let a CM field $E$ act on $A$ up to isogeny. We write $e\mapsto\bar e$ for its complex conjugation. Suppose that a polarization $\lambda$ induces this involution on $E$. Put $$V=H_1(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q),\qquad \dim_E V=2s,\qquad
 g=\dim A=s[E:\mathbb Q],$$ where $s\geq 1$. We assume that the Hodge multiplicities at every embedding of $E$ are $(s,s)$. The polarization is an alternating form $\psi:V\times V\longrightarrow\mathbb Q(1)$ satisfying $$\psi(ex,y)=\psi(x,\bar e y).$$

<a id="stmt-def-weil-space"></a>
<div id="def:weil-space" class="definition">

<a id="eq:weil-pure-space"></a>
**Definition 16**. The rational space of *Weil classes* associated with $E$ is the subspace $$W_E^s(A)\subset
 \bigwedge\nolimits_{\mathbb Q}^{2s} V^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q(s)
       =H^{2s}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(s))$$ whose scalar extension to a splitting field $\Omega$ of $E$ is $$\label{eq:weil-pure-space}
 W_E^s(A)\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega
 =\bigoplus_{\tau:E\hookrightarrow\Omega}
       \bigwedge\nolimits_\Omega^{2s}V_\tau^\vee
          \otimes\mathbb Q(s)_\Omega.$$ Here each summand is extended by zero on the other eigenspaces. The right side is Galois stable, so it defines the indicated rational subspace.

</div>

The balanced multiplicities imply that every element of $W_E^s(A)$ is a Hodge class: each determinant in <a href="#eq:weil-pure-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-pure-space">[eq:weil-pure-space]</a> has untwisted type $(s,s)$. We use its compatible realizations as an absolute Hodge class; see <a id="pm-cite-89"></a>[[Deligne1982](#ref-Deligne1982)]. The same eigenspace description of its support holds after specialization, since the $E$-action specializes compatibly.

<a id="eq:weil-U"></a>
Our goal is the Lefschetz pairing property for these specialized classes. In fact, the reduction satisfies a dichotomy determined by its rational endomorphisms. Let $\dagger$ be Rosati for the reduced polarization and put $$\label{eq:weil-U}
 \mathcal U=
 \{u\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A_0):u^\dagger=u,\quad
                      ue=\bar e u\ \text{for every }e\in E\}.$$

<a id="stmt-prop-weil"></a>
<div id="prop:weil" class="proposition">

**Proposition 17**. *Every $\gamma\in W_E^s(A)$ satisfies the Lefschetz pairing property at $w$. More precisely, if $\mathcal U$ in <a href="#eq:weil-U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-U">[eq:weil-U]</a> has no invertible element, all complementary pairings are zero. If it has an invertible element, $\gamma_0$ is a simultaneous rational Lefschetz class.*

</div>

We first prove the vanishing alternative. For the other alternative, an invertible $u$ supplies a divisor whose form is nondegenerate on each $E$-eigenspace separately. We will lift this divisor and use it to obtain a rational identity for the determinant classes.

<a id="pm-h-20"></a>
#### The orthogonality alternative

<a id="stmt-lem-weil-orthogonality"></a>
<div id="lem:weil-orthogonality" class="lemma">

**Lemma 18**. *If $\mathcal U$ contains no invertible element, every specialization of a class in $W_E^s(A)$ is orthogonal to all complementary Lefschetz classes, in each cohomological realization.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-weil-orthogonality"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weil-orthogonality</summary>

*Proof.* We prove the contrapositive. A nonzero pairing will produce a divisor form nondegenerate on one $E$-eigenspace. We will then show that a rational endomorphism in $\mathcal U$ with this property is invertible.

Fix one realization and extend its coefficient field to an algebraically closed field $\Omega$. At the crystalline component we also include the coefficient extension used for the specialized class. In this proof, $V_\tau$ also denotes the corresponding eigenspace in the dual of first cohomology of $A_0$, identified by specialization and comparison. Write $L^j_\Omega(A_0)$ for the $\Omega$-span of products of $j$ divisor classes. Since $[E:\mathbb Q]\geq2$, we have $g-2s\geq0$. Hard Lefschetz on the Lefschetz algebra gives <a id="eq:weil-hard-lefschetz"></a>$$\label{eq:weil-hard-lefschetz}
 L^{g-s}_\Omega(A_0)
        =\psi_0^{g-2s}L^s_\Omega(A_0).$$ The restriction of hard Lefschetz to this algebra, including for crystalline cohomology, follows from the Lefschetz correspondences of <a id="pm-cite-90"></a>[[Milne1999](#ref-Milne1999), Theorem 5.9].

Suppose a Weil class pairs nontrivially with $D_1\cdots D_s\psi_0^{g-2s}$, and choose a pure term, supported on $V_\tau$, whose contribution is nonzero. This term already has degree $2s$ on $V_\tau$, so no further factor in a nonzero top-degree product can use that space. A factor from $\psi_0$ which uses $V_{\bar\tau}$ also uses $V_\tau$; hence the polarization factors contribute to neither space in this pair. All $2s$ degrees on $V_{\bar\tau}$ must therefore come from the $s$ divisor factors. Each has degree two, so each must contribute its restriction to $V_{\bar\tau}$, and those restrictions have nonzero product. The coefficient of $t_1\cdots t_s$ in $$(t_1D_1+\cdots+t_sD_s)^s\big|_{V_{\bar\tau}}$$ is $s!$ times that product. Some $\Omega$-linear combination of the divisors therefore restricts to a nondegenerate alternating form on $V_{\bar\tau}$.

<a id="eq:weil-block-projection"></a>
We identify which endomorphisms can give these restrictions. Let $p_\xi\in E\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega$ be the eigenspace projectors. Rosati satisfies $p_\xi^\dagger=p_{\bar\xi}$. A divisor corresponds under $\psi_0$ to a Rosati-symmetric endomorphism $t$. Define $$\label{eq:weil-block-projection}
 \Pi(t)=\sum_\xi p_{\bar\xi}t p_\xi.$$ This operation takes place inside $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A_0)\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega$. Moreover, $$(p_{\bar\xi}t p_\xi)^\dagger
       =p_{\bar\xi}t^\dagger p_\xi,
 \qquad
 \Pi(t)e=\bar e\Pi(t).$$ Thus $\Pi(t)\in\mathcal U\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega$. Conversely, these are exactly the blocks allowed by the second equation in <a href="#eq:weil-U" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-U">[eq:weil-U]</a>. For $x,y\in V_\xi$, the form $\psi_0(x,ty)$ depends only on $p_{\bar\xi}t p_\xi$. It follows that projecting by <a href="#eq:weil-block-projection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-block-projection">[eq:weil-block-projection]</a> preserves all the same-embedding restrictions in question.

Nondegeneracy on $V_{\bar\tau}$ is a polynomial open condition on $\mathcal U\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega$, and we have shown this open is nonempty. The rational points of the rational vector space $\mathcal U$ are Zariski dense after extension to $\Omega$.
