Hence there is a rational $u\in\mathcal U$ for which $\psi_0(\cdot,u\cdot)$ is nondegenerate on $V_{\bar\tau}$.

To prove invertibility, we use the fact that a nonzero $E$-stable abelian subvariety has a nonzero homology space at every embedding of $E$. Suppose that $u$ is not invertible, and choose an integer $N>0$ such that $Nu$ is an endomorphism. The reduced identity component $C=((\ker(Nu))^0)_{\mathrm{red}}$ is a positive-dimensional abelian subvariety. The relation $ue=\bar e u$ makes $C$ stable under the rational action of $E$. This action is unital and therefore faithful. Choose a primitive element $e$ of $E$ and an integer $M>0$ such that $Me$ acts by an integral endomorphism of $C$. The exterior algebra description of cohomology and normalized top-degree pullback give, in every realization <a id="pm-cite-91"></a>[[Milne1999](#ref-Milne1999), Appendix, Proposition (A.2)(a),(b)], $$\det\bigl(n-e\mid H^1(C)\bigr)
    =M^{-2\dim C}\deg\bigl([Mn]-Me\bigr)$$ for every integer $n$ for which the map on the right is an isogeny. All but finitely many integers have this property. The right side is rational and independent of the realization. Polynomial interpolation therefore gives one characteristic polynomial in $\mathbb Q[T]$, identical also in crystalline cohomology. Its roots lie among the conjugates of $e$, and rationality forces their multiplicities to be equal. Since $C\ne0$, every embedding of $E$ occurs with positive multiplicity. The same statement holds on first homology, in the chosen realization. Rational Poincaré reducibility <a id="pm-cite-92"></a>[[MilneAV](#ref-MilneAV), Proposition 12.1] makes the inclusion $C\hookrightarrow A_0$ injective on first homology in every realization. This inclusion is $E$-equivariant, and its image is killed by $u$. Thus the nonzero $\bar\tau$-eigenspace of $H_1(C)$ shows that the kernel of $u$ meets $V_{\bar\tau}$ nontrivially. Such a vector lies in the radical of $\psi_0(\cdot,u\cdot)|_{V_{\bar\tau}}$, a contradiction.

A nonzero pairing would therefore give an invertible element of $\mathcal U$. Taking the contrapositive proves the lemma. The argument applies separately to every realization, so all the pairings in this case have the common rational value zero. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-21"></a>
#### A divisor identity on a rational summand

<a id="eq:weil-extra-divisor"></a>
For the rest of the section, suppose that $u\in\mathcal U$ is invertible. It gives a rational divisor $D^u$ on $A_0$ with alternating form $$\label{eq:weil-extra-divisor}
 D^u(x,y)=\psi_0(x,uy).$$ This form is nondegenerate and pairs each embedding of $E$ only with itself: $u$ exchanges opposite eigenspaces, whereas $\psi_0$ pairs opposite eigenspaces.

Prescribed-filtration transport will replace $A_0$ by a power and then by an isogenous special fiber $B_0$. We will retain the first factor through a rational projector on $B_0$, and lift that projector, the $E$-action on its image, and the transported divisor to $B$. The next lemma explains the resulting divisor identity: a divisor nondegenerate separately on those eigenspaces generates their determinant classes. A rational projector here means an idempotent in the rational endomorphism algebra.

<a id="stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span"></a>
<div id="lem:weil-divisor-span" class="lemma">

**Lemma 19**. *Let $B$ be a complex abelian variety, let $R\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(B)$ be a rational projector, and suppose that there is an algebra action $E\longrightarrow R\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(B)R$ whose unit maps to $R$. Let $T=\operatorname{im}(R)$ in rational first homology, with $\dim_E T=2s$; the indicated endomorphisms act by zero on $\ker(R)$. Suppose there is a rational divisor class $D$ on $B$ such that $R^*D=D$ and, after splitting $E$, its alternating form is $$D=\sum_\tau D_\tau,
 \qquad D_\tau\in\bigwedge\nolimits^2 T_\tau^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q(1),$$ with each $D_\tau$ nondegenerate. Then the rational subspace whose scalar extension is $$\bigoplus_\tau\bigwedge\nolimits^{2s}T_\tau^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q(s),$$ extended by zero on the complementary summand, consists of rational Lefschetz classes on $B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weil-divisor-span</summary>

<a id="eq:weil-coefficient-polynomial"></a>
*Proof.* For $b\in E$, let $\widetilde b\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(B)$ act by $b$ on $T$ and by zero on the complement. Its pullback of a rational divisor is a rational divisor: clear a denominator in $\widetilde b$, and use that multiplication by $N$ acts as $N^2$ on $H^2$. Over a splitting field $\Omega$, choosing Tate-line bases, we have $$(\widetilde b^*D)^s
       =\left(\sum_\tau\tau(b)^2D_\tau\right)^s.$$ The coordinates $x_\tau=\tau(b)$ are independent coordinates on the scalar extension of the rational vector group underlying $E$. In the corresponding vector-valued polynomial $$\label{eq:weil-coefficient-polynomial}
 P((x_\tau)_\tau)=\left(\sum_\tau x_\tau^2D_\tau\right)^s,$$ the coefficient of $x_\tau^{2s}$ is $D_\tau^s$. Nondegeneracy makes this coefficient nonzero, so it spans $\bigwedge^{2s}T_\tau^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q(s)_\Omega$.

Let $M$ be the rational span of the Lefschetz classes $(\widetilde b^*D)^s$ for $b\in E$. The set $E$ of rational points is Zariski dense in its vector group, also after extension to $\Omega$. Any linear functional annihilating $M\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega$ annihilates all evaluations of <a href="#eq:weil-coefficient-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-coefficient-polynomial">[eq:weil-coefficient-polynomial]</a> on those points. It therefore annihilates the polynomial, and hence each coefficient. Every $D_\tau^s$ belongs to $M\otimes_\mathbb Q\Omega$.

This proves the required inclusion after scalar extension. Both subspaces are rational; applying faithful scalar extension to their quotient proves the inclusion over $\mathbb Q$. In particular it gives rational coefficients for every rational class in the asserted subspace, without requiring an individual embedding line to be defined over $\mathbb Q$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-22"></a>
#### A lift preserving the divisor

<a id="eq:weil-group"></a>
We now construct the auxiliary variety to which Lemma <a href="#lem:weil-divisor-span" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weil-divisor-span">19</a> applies. To transport the Weil class, we use the $E$-linear polarization similitudes whose determinant has the same transformation law as a codimension-$s$ Tate twist. Choose a rational basis for the Betti Tate line, let $c$ be the multiplier of $\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\psi)$, and set $$\label{eq:weil-group}
 G=\{a\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}(V,\psi)\cap\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_E(V):
                         \det_E(a)=c(a)^s\}.$$ On points over a $\mathbb Q$-algebra $R$, the determinant equality is in $(E\otimes_\mathbb QR)^\times$, with the right side embedded diagonally.

<a id="stmt-lem-weil-group"></a>
<div id="lem:weil-group" class="lemma">

**Lemma 20**. *The Hodge homomorphism of $A$ factors through $G$, and its conjugacy class defines a Shimura datum of Hodge type through the displayed embedding. Over an algebraically closed coefficient field, the orbit of the corresponding filtration on $H^1$ consists exactly of the $E$-stable Lagrangians for the dual polarization pairing whose dimension at each embedding of $E$ is $s$. Using a basis of its Tate line, every alternating form in <a href="#eq:weil-pure-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-pure-space">[eq:weil-pure-space]</a> transforms by $c^s$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-weil-group"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weil-group</summary>

*Proof.* The polarization pairs $V_\tau$ with $V_{\bar\tau}$, and no other pair of eigenspaces. Over an algebraically closed field, choose one embedding in each conjugate pair. An element of $G$ is specified by $c$ and matrices $a_\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_{2s}$ with $\det(a_\tau)=c^s$; the matrices on the opposite spaces are then determined by the polarization. In particular, $$\ker(c:G\longrightarrow\mathbb G_m)
       \simeq\prod_{\{\tau,\bar\tau\}}\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_{2s}.$$ The multiplier is surjective geometrically. Thus $G$ is connected reductive, with the same adjoint group as the group of $E$-linear polarization similitudes.

The two Hodge characters occur $s$ times at every embedding. Their product is the polarization multiplier, so the Hodge homomorphism satisfies the determinant condition in <a href="#eq:weil-group" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-group">[eq:weil-group]</a>. Its weight is rational and central. On the adjoint Lie algebra the Hodge types are among $(-1,1),(0,0),(1,-1)$, as follows from the weight-one representation. Conjugation by the image of $i$ is a Cartan involution, by the polarization. At every real embedding of the maximal totally real subfield of $E$, the adjoint group has unitary signature $(s,s)$. Its Hodge homomorphism is nontrivial there, so no rational simple adjoint factor has trivial Hodge homomorphism. These are the Shimura-datum conditions for the given Hodge embedding.

On $H^1$ the polarization still pairs opposite eigenspaces. An $E$-stable Lagrangian is specified by an $s$-dimensional subspace at one embedding of each pair; the subspace at the opposite embedding is its annihilator. The factor $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_{2s}$ acts transitively on these subspaces, as in the cohomological orbit calculation of Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a>. Finally, an alternating determinant form evaluated on $a_\tau v_1,\ldots,a_\tau v_{2s}$ is multiplied by $\det(a_\tau)=c^s$. This proves the asserted transformation law. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-weil-group-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weil-group</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:weil" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:weil">17</a>.* The vanishing alternative is Lemma <a href="#lem:weil-orthogonality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weil-orthogonality">18</a>. Suppose that $u$ is invertible, and use the divisor $D^u$ from <a href="#eq:weil-extra-divisor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weil-extra-divisor">[eq:weil-extra-divisor]</a>.

*A filtration preserving the divisor.*

Let $\mathcal D=H^1_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p]$, with its contravariant convention, and let $\beta$ be the dual polarization pairing, taking values in the isocrystal line of slope one. The actions on $\mathcal D$ satisfy $$\beta(ux,y)=\beta(x,uy),\qquad
 \beta(ex,y)=\beta(x,\bar e y),\qquad ue=\bar e u.$$ All these endomorphisms and the polarization have finite field descent. Theorem <a href="#thm:filtration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:filtration">10</a> supplies, over a finite extension with finite local field descent, a weakly admissible filtration $\mathop{\mathrm{Fil}}^1\mathcal D=L$ which is $E$- and $u$-stable, is Lagrangian for $\beta$, and has dimension $s$ at every embedding of $E$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:weil-group" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weil-group">20</a> it lies geometrically in the required $G$-orbit. The [finite-descent argument](#par:transport-tensor-descent) in Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a> shows that its full finite family of Hodge-type structure tensors descends from the original number-field point by crystalline comparison. Taking a compositum with the local field of definition of $L$ gives the common finite local-field descent required by its prescribed-filtration clause.

*Transport and the first rational summand.* Apply Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a> to $G$ and $\gamma$ with this filtration. It gives a lift $B$, a quasi-isogeny $a:A_0^n\longrightarrow B_0$, and a rational Hodge class $\kappa$ on $B$, with $$f_0=aj:A_0\longrightarrow B_0,\qquad
 f_0^*\kappa_0=\gamma_0,$$ simultaneously in the realizations under consideration. The filtration on $B$, marked by $a$, is the direct sum of $n$ copies of the chosen filtration. Here $j$ includes the first factor. The map $f_0$ identifies $A_0$ with a rational direct summand of $B_0$. Its retraction $q_0$ and the corresponding projector $R_0$ are <a id="eq:weil-projector-identity"></a>$$\label{eq:weil-projector-identity}
 q_0=\operatorname{pr}_1a^{-1},\qquad R_0=f_0q_0.$$ Thus $q_0f_0=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ and $R_0^2=R_0$. Pulling back $f_0^*\kappa_0=\gamma_0$ by $q_0$ gives $$R_0^*\kappa_0=q_0^*\gamma_0.$$ Act by $E$ on $\operatorname{im}(R_0)$ using $e\mapsto f_0e q_0$, and by zero on the complement. Also put $D_0=q_0^*D^u$.

*Lifting the projector, action, and divisor.* The projector and this $E$-action preserve the repeated filtration. By Corollary <a href="#cor:filtration-divisor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:filtration-divisor">15</a>, $D^u$ satisfies the divisor lifting condition for the chosen filtration on the first summand. The crystalline map induced by $q_0=\operatorname{pr}_1a^{-1}$ respects that filtration and the marked repeated filtration on $B_0$. Hence $D_0=q_0^*D^u$ satisfies the same condition on $B$. Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:lift-hom" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lift-hom">5</a> to $R_0$, to a finite $\mathbb Q$-basis of the action of $E$ on $\operatorname{im}(R_0)$, and to the symmetric homomorphism $h_{D_0}:B_0\to B_0^\vee$ defined by $D_0$. Denote their lifts by $R$, the corresponding basis of maps, and $h_D$. Injectivity of specialization preserves $R^2=R$, the rational multiplication table of that basis, and its unit $R$. It therefore gives an algebra action $E\to R\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(B)R$. The symmetric map $h_D$ defines a rational divisor class $D$. Moreover, $q_0R_0=q_0$, so $$R_0^\vee h_{D_0}R_0=h_{D_0}.$$ Injectivity lifts this identity to $R^\vee h_DR=h_D$, which is exactly $R^*D=D$. Thus the required projector, action, and divisor all exist on the one auxiliary variety $B$.

*The rational identity and its specialization.* Consider now rational Betti cohomology at a complex realization of $B$. The summand $T=\operatorname{im}(R)$ has dimension $2s$ at each embedding of $E$. The form of $D$ is supported on this summand and pairs each embedding with itself nondegenerately. Also $R^*\kappa$ is supported on the pure determinant spaces of this summand. These assertions can be checked after extension to any one prime-to-$p$ realization: for the divisor they follow from its specified specialization, and for the Hodge class they follow from the equality $R_0^*\kappa_0=q_0^*\gamma_0$ and the pure support of $\gamma_0$. Compatibility of the transported realizations then gives these support and nondegeneracy assertions in Betti cohomology.

Lemma <a href="#lem:weil-divisor-span" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:weil-divisor-span">19</a> now expresses the rational Betti class $R^*\kappa$ as a finite rational linear combination of products of rational divisor classes on $B$. The realization and specialization compatibilities in Proposition <a href="#prop:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:transport">6</a>, together with those for divisors and homomorphisms, specialize this identity with the same rational coefficients in every component. Hence $R_0^*\kappa_0$ is a simultaneous rational Lefschetz class. Finally, $$\gamma_0=f_0^*R_0^*\kappa_0,$$ because $R_0f_0=f_0$. Pullback by a rational homomorphism preserves simultaneous rational Lefschetz classes, so the same is true of $\gamma_0$. Its complementary traces are therefore one common rational intersection number, proving the second alternative and the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:reduction"></a>
### CM reduction and consequences

The remaining step in the main proof is to express CM Hodge classes as pullbacks of balanced Weil classes. Together with CM transport and Proposition <a href="#prop:weil" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:weil">17</a>, this will prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. We then derive two specialization consequences.

<a id="pm-h-24"></a>
#### CM classes as pullbacks of Weil classes

We give the CM reduction in the form needed here. The stronger reduction to classes on abelian varieties of split Weil type is due to Deligne and André <a id="pm-cite-93"></a>[[Andre1992](#ref-Andre1992)]; see the reconstruction in <a id="pm-cite-94"></a>[[Milne2020](#ref-Milne2020), Theorem 1 and Section 4]. Only balanced signatures and a compatible polarization are needed for our application, and we prove this form directly using rational idempotents on powers.

<a id="stmt-lem-cm-reduction"></a>
<div id="lem:cm-reduction" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:cm-pullback-decomposition"></a>
**Lemma 21** (CM reduction). *Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of CM type with good reduction at $w$, and let $r>0$. For every rational Hodge class $\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$, there are finitely many abelian varieties $B_i/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$, rational homomorphisms $f_i\colon A\to B_i$, and rational Weil classes $\eta_i\in H^{2r}(B_i(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$ such that $$\label{eq:cm-pullback-decomposition}
                 \gamma=\sum_i f_i^*\eta_i.$$ Each $B_i$ has an action of a CM field $K$ for which $H_1(B_i(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q)$ has rank $2r$ over $K$, the Hodge signatures are $(r,r)$ at every embedding of $K$, and a polarization induces complex conjugation on $K$. The equality specializes simultaneously in all the realizations used in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cm-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cm-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* We first identify the eigenspace components that can support a rational Hodge class. For each such component we construct a balanced abelian factor of a power of $A$, whose Weil classes pull back onto that component. Taking the sum of these rational pullback spaces will then give the decomposition over $\mathbb Q$.

*Balanced eigenspace components.* Set $V=H_1(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q)$. We work throughout up to isogeny. There is a CM algebra $E'$, a product of CM fields, acting on $A$ such that $V$ is free of rank one over $E'$. To obtain it, decompose $A$ up to isogeny into simple CM factors and take the product of their CM fields, with a separate label for each repeated factor. Transporting this action back along an isogeny gives the asserted action on $A$ itself.

Choose a finite Galois CM field $K\subset\overline{\mathbb Q}$ containing the images of all the factors of $E'$, and put $S=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb Q\text{-alg}}(E',K)$. Then $$V_K:=V\otimes_{\mathbb Q}K=\bigoplus_{\rho\in S}V_\rho,
                \qquad \dim_K V_\rho=1.$$ The Hodge decomposition is diagonal for this decomposition after extension to $\mathbb C$. We suppress the common Tate twist in the following linear-algebra calculations. Alternating forms of degree $2r$ decompose as $$\bigwedge^{2r}V_K^*
     =\bigoplus_{\substack{\Delta\subset S\\ |\Delta|=2r}}L_\Delta,
 \qquad
 L_\Delta=
 \bigwedge^{2r}\left(\bigoplus_{\rho\in\Delta}V_\rho\right)^*,$$ where each form on the right is extended by zero on the other summands. Every $L_\Delta$ is one-dimensional and has a definite Hodge type.

Suppose the component of $\gamma$ in $L_\Delta$ is nonzero. Since $\gamma$ is rational, its component in $L_{\sigma\Delta}$ is the semilinear conjugate of that component and is nonzero for every $\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/\mathbb Q)$. The Hodge condition therefore implies that every $\sigma\Delta$ contains $r$ lines of each of the two Hodge types. We call such a subset balanced under all conjugates.

*An abelian factor with balanced signatures.* Fix one of these subsets $\Delta$. Form the abelian variety $A\otimes_{\mathbb Q}K$ in the category up to isogeny: it is a power of $A$ whose index space is the underlying rational vector space of $K$. The regular action of $K$ on this index space is an action by rational endomorphisms of the power. Its rational homology is $$T=V\otimes_{\mathbb Q}K,\qquad
 \dim_\mathbb QT=[K:\mathbb Q]\dim_\mathbb QV.$$ In contrast with the coefficient space $V_K$, we regard $T$ here as a $\mathbb Q$-vector space, with $K$ acting on its second factor. The abelian variety remains over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$. The actions on the two factors give an action of the rational algebra $$E'\otimes_{\mathbb Q}K\simeq\prod_{\rho\in S}K,
 \qquad e\otimes k\longmapsto\bigl(\rho(e)k\bigr)_{\rho\in S}.$$ Let $e_\Delta$ be the idempotent with coordinates $1$ on $\Delta$ and $0$ elsewhere. It is a rational endomorphism of $A\otimes_{\mathbb Q}K$, so its image is an abelian variety $B_\Delta$ up to isogeny. In particular no Galois invariance of $\Delta$ is required for this construction. Write $$U=H_1(B_\Delta(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q)=e_\Delta T.$$ The index-field action of $K$ preserves $U$.

Now extend the coefficients of $U$ to $K$, and use the fixed inclusion $K\subset\mathbb C$ to identify the embeddings of $K$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(K/\mathbb Q)$. Evaluation of the index factor at $\tau$ identifies its eigenspace in $T\otimes_\mathbb QK$ with $V_K$. In that eigenspace the idempotent retains exactly the summands indexed by $\tau\Delta$. Thus <a id="eq:cm-embedding-summand"></a>$$\label{eq:cm-embedding-summand}
                  U_\tau=\bigoplus_{\rho\in\Delta}V_{\tau\rho},$$ and the same description holds after extension to $\mathbb C$. Explicitly, evaluation of the idempotent at the pair of embeddings $(\xi,\tau)$ is $1$ exactly when $\xi=\tau\rho$ for some $\rho\in\Delta$. Thus $\dim_K U_\tau=2r$ for every $\tau$. Since $K$ is Galois and $\Delta$ is balanced under all conjugates, each $U_\tau$ has $r$ lines of each Hodge type. Consequently $U$ has rank $2r$ over $K$, $\dim B_\Delta=r[K:\mathbb Q]$, and the required Hodge signatures are $(r,r)$.

*A compatible polarization.* Let $\psi$ be a rational polarization form on $V$, and equip the index space $K$ with the positive definite rational symmetric form $$t(k,l)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{K/\mathbb Q}(k\bar l).$$ The form $\Psi=\psi\otimes t$ is a rational polarization form on the power $A\otimes_{\mathbb Q}K$: its alternating and Hodge properties follow from those of $\psi$, and its positivity follows by tensoring the positive symmetric form associated to $\psi$ with $t$. A positive integral multiple gives an actual polarization. For $a\in K$, $$t(ak,l)=t(k,\bar a l),$$ so the Rosati involution of $\Psi$ induces conjugation on the index-field action. Restrict $\Psi$ to $B_\Delta$. This is again a polarization, and because $U$ is $K$-stable its adjoint identity still induces conjugation on $K$. This restriction does not require $e_\Delta$ to be self-adjoint.

*Pullback and descent of the rational span.* The factor $B_\Delta$ now has the field action, balanced signatures, and polarization needed to define its Weil classes. The rational homomorphism $f_\Delta\colon A\to B_\Delta$ defined on homology by $$v\longmapsto e_\Delta(v\otimes 1)$$ has the needed effect on Weil classes. After splitting coefficients, its component at the identity embedding of $K$ is the projection $$\bigoplus_{\rho\in S}V_\rho
                    \longrightarrow
                 \bigoplus_{\rho\in\Delta}V_\rho.$$ Thus pullback maps $\bigwedge^{2r}U_{\mathop{\mathrm{id}}}^*$ isomorphically onto $L_\Delta$. More generally the component indexed by $\tau$ pulls back onto $L_{\tau\Delta}$. The rational Weil-class space $W_K^r(B_\Delta)$ is characterized, with the twist restored, by $$W_K^r(B_\Delta)\otimes_{\mathbb Q}K
       =\bigoplus_{\tau:K\hookrightarrow K}
                         \bigwedge^{2r}U_\tau^*(r).$$ In particular $f_\Delta^*W_K^r(B_\Delta)$ is a rational subspace whose extension to $K$ contains $L_\Delta(r)$.

Take the sum of these rational pullback spaces over the finitely many subsets supporting $\gamma$. Its scalar extension contains every component of $\gamma$, and therefore contains $\gamma$ itself. Since extension of scalars is injective on the quotient by a rational subspace, the sum already contains $\gamma$ over $\mathbb Q$. This proves <a href="#eq:cm-pullback-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-pullback-decomposition">[eq:cm-pullback-decomposition]</a> with rational Weil classes. Notice that an individual embedding component need not descend to $\mathbb Q$; only the entire Weil-class space and its pullback are used in this descent argument.

*Good reduction and specialization.* All the constructions use finitely many rational endomorphisms of powers of $A$ and their images. They therefore exist over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ and descend, together with the maps, to a common number field after a finite extension. Choose that extension so that $A$ has good reduction at the place specified by $w$. For any $\ell\ne p$, the rational Tate module of $B_\Delta$ is a Galois-stable direct summand of the rational Tate module of the power of $A$. It is unramified at this place. The Néron–Ogg–Shafarevich criterion gives good reduction of $B_\Delta$ there; it also shows that the isogenies used in the construction do not affect this conclusion <a id="pm-cite-95"></a>[[MilneAVNotes](#ref-MilneAVNotes), Chapter IV, Theorem 3.5]. Rational homomorphisms between the resulting good-reduction varieties specialize after clearing denominators. The equality of rational Hodge classes <a href="#eq:cm-pullback-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-pullback-decomposition">[eq:cm-pullback-decomposition]</a> is respected by their compatible realizations and by specialization, with the same rational coefficients in every component. This proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-cm-pairing"></a>
<div id="cor:cm-pairing" class="corollary">

**Corollary 22**. *The Lefschetz pairing property holds for the specialization of every rational Hodge class on a CM abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-cm-pairing"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-cm-pairing</summary>

*Proof.* In codimension zero the class is a rational multiple of the unit, so the assertion follows from the compatibility of divisor intersection numbers with the normalized traces. In positive codimension use Lemma <a href="#lem:cm-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cm-reduction">21</a>. Proposition <a href="#prop:weil" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:weil">17</a> gives the Lefschetz pairing property for the specialization of each $\eta_i$. Lemma <a href="#lem:pairing-functorial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pairing-functorial">4</a> preserves this property under rational pullback and rational linear combinations. Applying it to <a href="#eq:cm-pullback-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cm-pullback-decomposition">[eq:cm-pullback-decomposition]</a> proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-25"></a>
#### Proof of the main theorem

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-cm-pairing-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-cm-pairing</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* The case $r=0$ follows from divisor intersection numbers as in the preceding corollary. This includes $\dim A=0$, where the abelian variety is a point. If $r=\dim A$, a rational top class is a rational multiple of the point class; its normalized trace is preserved by specialization, and the complementary product is $1$. We may therefore assume $0<r<\dim A$.

Corollary <a href="#cor:cm-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cm-transport">9</a> gives a CM abelian variety $B/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with good reduction at $w$, a rational Hodge class $\kappa$ on $B$, a quasi-isogeny $a\colon A_0^n\to B_0$, and the first-factor inclusion $j\colon A_0\to A_0^n$, such that $$\gamma_0=(aj)^*\kappa_0$$ simultaneously in all the realizations under consideration. Corollary <a href="#cor:cm-pairing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cm-pairing">22</a> gives the Lefschetz pairing property for $\kappa_0$, and Lemma <a href="#lem:pairing-functorial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pairing-functorial">4</a> gives it for $\gamma_0$. Hence, for each prescribed product of complementary divisor classes on $A_0$, all the normalized traces in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> equal one rational number. This proves the theorem. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-26"></a>
#### Specialization in a fixed good theory

We now use CM transport to place the specialization of every rational Hodge class in one fixed theory of rational Tate classes.

Continue to fix $\overline{\mathbb Q}\subset\mathbb C$, $p$, and $w$, with $\mathbb F$, $K_0$, and $C_w$ as in the introduction. Let $\mathcal S_0$ be Milne’s class of $\mathbb F$-varieties whose connected components are products of abelian varieties and projective spaces. The CM case of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, by linearity for complementary Lefschetz classes, satisfies the hypothesis of <a id="pm-cite-96"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), Theorem 4.5]. Fix one good theory $\mathcal R$ of rational Tate classes on $\mathcal S_0$ supplied by that theorem, before varying any abelian variety or Hodge class.

For $\rho\in\mathcal R^r(X)$, write $(\rho)_\ell$ for its $\ell$-adic component when $\ell\ne p$, and $(\rho)_p$ for its native crystalline Tate component in $$H^{2r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/W(\mathbb F))[1/p](r).$$ We use three properties of the theory. Its spaces are rational vector spaces preserved by pullback, its realization maps are injective, and specializations of CM Hodge classes belong to it. These are the properties in axioms <span class="upright">(R1)</span>, <span class="upright">(R4)</span>, and the goodness axiom <span class="upright">(R3)</span>, respectively <a id="pm-cite-97"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), Definitions 2.1 and 3.1]. The following consequence extends the last property to all rational Hodge classes, while keeping this one theory fixed.

<a id="stmt-cor-all-hodge-specialization"></a>
<div id="cor:all-hodge-specialization" class="corollary">

**Corollary 23** (Specialization of all rational Hodge classes). *For every abelian variety $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ of dimension $d$ with good reduction $A_0/\mathbb F$ at $w$, every $0\leq r\leq d$, and every rational Hodge class $$\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r)),$$ there is a unique class $\rho_\gamma\in\mathcal R^r(A_0)$ such that $$(\rho_\gamma)_\ell=\gamma_{0,\ell}\quad(\ell\ne p),\qquad
 (\rho_\gamma)_p\otimes 1=\gamma_{0,p}.$$ The prime-to-$p$ equalities are in $H^{2r}_{\acute{e}t}(A_0,\mathbb Q_\ell(r))$; the crystalline equality is in $$H^{2r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p](r)
       \otimes_{K_0}C_w.$$ The specialization components are those of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. The assertion holds for every prime $p$, including $p=2$, with the prime, place, and theory fixed before $A$ and $\gamma$ vary.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-all-hodge-specialization"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-all-hodge-specialization</summary>

*Proof.* In codimension zero, take the corresponding rational multiple of the unit, which belongs to the theory by pullback from the point. This also covers $d=0$. In top codimension $r=d>0$, take $q$ times the point class, where $q\in\mathbb Q$ is the normalized trace of $\gamma$. The point class belongs to $\mathcal R^d(A_0)$ <a id="pm-cite-98"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), 2.3], and its normalized trace is one. Compatibility of traces with specialization gives the required components in these cases.

Suppose now that $0<r<d$. Choose the CM variety $B$, Hodge class $\kappa$, and rational homomorphism $$b=aj\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^0(A_0,B_0),\qquad
 \gamma_0=b^*\kappa_0$$ from Corollary <a href="#cor:cm-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cm-transport">9</a>. The equality holds in all the stated realizations, with the usual absolute-Hodge specializations of $\kappa$. Axiom <span class="upright">(R3)</span> gives one class $\rho_\kappa\in\mathcal R^r(B_0)$ with $$(\rho_\kappa)_\ell=\kappa_{0,\ell}\quad(\ell\ne p),\qquad
 (\rho_\kappa)_p\otimes1=\kappa_{0,p}.$$

Choose an integer $N>0$ such that $h=Nb$ is an actual homomorphism $A_0\to B_0$. Pullback by $h$ preserves the theory by axiom <span class="upright">(R1)</span> of <a id="pm-cite-99"></a>[[Milne2009](#ref-Milne2009), Definition 2.1]. Since $\mathcal R^r(A_0)$ is a $\mathbb Q$-vector space, we may set $$\rho_\gamma=N^{-2r}h^*\rho_\kappa\in\mathcal R^r(A_0).$$ Functoriality of the component maps and <a href="#eq:rational-pullback" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rational-pullback">[eq:rational-pullback]</a> give $$\begin{aligned}
 (\rho_\gamma)_\ell
   &=N^{-2r}h^*\kappa_{0,\ell}
     =b^*\kappa_{0,\ell}=\gamma_{0,\ell}
       &&(\ell\ne p),\\
 (\rho_\gamma)_p\otimes1
   &=N^{-2r}h^*\kappa_{0,p}
     =b^*\kappa_{0,p}=\gamma_{0,p}.
\end{aligned}$$ The second line is taken after extension from $K_0$ to $C_w$. Pullback in <span class="upright">(R1)</span> applies to any homomorphism $h$, and rational scalar multiplication allows any denominator $N$. Thus the argument requires neither an isogeny nor separability, and remains valid when $p\mid N$, including at $p=2$.

Finally, two classes with the required components have the same $\ell$-adic component for any one $\ell\ne p$. Injectivity of that component map by <span class="upright">(R4)</span> makes them equal. Thus the class is unique in the fixed theory, independently of the auxiliary choices. ◻

</details>

This cohomological membership statement alone gives no algebraic cycle and proves neither the Hodge nor the Tate conjecture. The construction takes place at the fixed reduction place: its CM auxiliary may have dimension $n\dim A$, and the map $b$ is between special fibers. It supplies no same-dimensional CM lift or map between the generic fibers.

<a id="pm-h-27"></a>
#### Algebraicity after good reduction

The CM Hodge theorem <a id="pm-cite-100"></a>[[OpenAICMHodge2026](#ref-OpenAICMHodge2026), Theorem 1.1], combined with our CM transport, strengthens the preceding specialization statement. It is an additional input to the following consequence, not to the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="stmt-cor-algebraic-specialization"></a>
<div id="cor:algebraic-specialization" class="corollary">

**Corollary 24** (Algebraic specialization of rational Hodge classes). *Fix the prime and place as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Let $A/\overline{\mathbb Q}$ be an abelian variety of dimension $d$ with good reduction $A_0/\mathbb F$ at $w$, and let $\gamma\in H^{2r}(A(\mathbb C),\mathbb Q(r))$ be a rational Hodge class, where $0\leq r\leq d$. There is one rational algebraic cycle $z_\gamma\in\operatorname{CH}^r(A_0)_{\mathbb Q}$ such that $$\operatorname{cl}_\ell(z_\gamma)=\gamma_{0,\ell}
       \quad(\ell\ne p),\qquad
 \operatorname{cl}_{\mathrm{cris}}(z_\gamma)\otimes1=\gamma_{0,p}.$$ The crystalline equality is in $$H^{2r}_{\mathrm{cris}}(A_0/W(\mathbb F))[1/p](r)
       \otimes_{K_0}C_w.$$ This holds for every prime $p$, including $p=2$. Consequently, for every $y\in\operatorname{CH}^{d-r}(A_0)_{\mathbb Q}$, the traces of $\gamma_{0,\ell}\operatorname{cl}_\ell(y)$ for $\ell\ne p$ and of $\gamma_{0,p}\operatorname{cl}_{\mathrm{cris}}(y)$ are all the same rational number $\deg(z_\gamma\cdot y)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-algebraic-specialization"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-algebraic-specialization</summary>

*Proof.* In codimension zero or top codimension, use the corresponding rational multiple of the unit or the point class, as in the preceding proof. This includes dimension zero. For $0<r<d$, use the CM variety $B$, rational Hodge class $\kappa$, and map $$b=aj\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}^0(A_0,B_0),\qquad \gamma_0=b^*\kappa_0$$ given by Corollary <a href="#cor:cm-transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cm-transport">9</a>, with the equality in every stated realization.

By <a id="pm-cite-101"></a>[[OpenAICMHodge2026](#ref-OpenAICMHodge2026), Theorem 1.1], $\kappa$ is the Betti class of a rational algebraic cycle on $B_{\mathbb C}$. A representative can be chosen over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$: spread each of its finitely many components in the Hilbert scheme of the fixed projective variety $B$, and choose a $\overline{\mathbb Q}$-point on the same geometrically connected component. The universal flat family preserves the Betti cycle class. Thus there is $Z\in\operatorname{CH}^r(B)_{\mathbb Q}$ with class $\kappa$. Descend $B$ and $Z$ to a number field and extend the chosen place so that $B$ has an abelian scheme model. Specialization gives $Z_0\in\operatorname{CH}^r(B_0)_{\mathbb Q}$. Compatibility of cycle classes with smooth proper specialization and the de Rham–crystalline comparison identifies its classes with $\kappa_{0,\ell}$ and $\kappa_{0,p}$, respectively. In particular the same rational cycle $Z_0$ gives every realization.

Choose $N>0$ with $h=Nb$ an actual homomorphism $A_0\to B_0$, and put $$z_\gamma=N^{-2r}h^*Z_0\in\operatorname{CH}^r(A_0)_{\mathbb Q}.$$ Here $h^*$ is the Chow pullback between smooth varieties, defined by the graph and refined intersection; it requires neither flatness nor separability of $h$. Functoriality of cycle classes and <a href="#eq:rational-pullback" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rational-pullback">[eq:rational-pullback]</a> give the required equalities. Rational coefficients permit this denominator even if $p\mid N$; the crystalline realization has already inverted $p$. Finally, compatibility of cycle classes with products and normalized traces gives the asserted intersection number for every $y$. ◻

</details>

This conclusion concerns only the special fiber at the fixed place. It neither asserts the Hodge conjecture for arbitrary abelian varieties in characteristic zero nor lifts $z_\gamma$ to the original generic fiber. The representing cycle is not asserted to be unique modulo rational equivalence, and no comparison between different reduction places is made.

<a id="pm-h-28"></a>
#### References

<div class="thebibliography">

Stacks

    {
      "caption": "The two uses of transport. Each lower horizontal arrow is one actual quasi-isogeny preserving the specified tensors in all realizations; the vertical arrows denote good reduction. There is no claimed map between the generic fibers. The upper construction starts from the original variety. The lower construction is applied separately to a balanced Weil variety $Z$ arising in the CM reduction; its lift $B$ realizes the chosen filtration $ℱ_{Z}^{⊕m}$ under $a_{fl}^{*}$. The CM and prescribed-filtration auxiliaries need not coincide.",

Top diagram titled \"CM transport\":\n- Top-left rectangular box containing \"$A^n/\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}$\".\n- Top-right rectangular box containing \"$C/\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}} \\text{ (CM)}$\".\n- Bottom-left rectangular box containing \"$A_0^n$\".\n- Bottom-right rectangular box containing \"$C_0$\".\n- Vertical downward arrow from $A^n/\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}}$ to $A_0^n$ labeled \"reduction\" on the left.\n- Vertical downward arrow from $C/\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}} \\text{ (CM)}$ to $C_0$ labeled \"reduction\" on the right.\n- Horizontal rightward arrow from $A_0^n$ to $C_0$ labeled \"$a_{\\mathrm{CM}}$\".\n- Centered formula below the lower boxes: \"$\\gamma_0 = (a_{\\mathrm{CM}} j)^* \\kappa_0$\".\n2. Bottom diagram titled \"Prescribed-filtration transport\":\n- Top-left rectangular box containing \"$Z^m/\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}} \\text{ (balanced Weil)}$\".\n- Top-right rectangular box containing \"$B/K, K/K_0 \\text{ finite}$\".\n- Bottom-left rectangular box containing \"$Z_0^m$\".\n- Bottom-right rectangular box containing \"$B_0$\".\n- Vertical downward arrow from $Z^m/\\overline{\\mathbb{Q}} \\text{ (balanced Weil)}$ to $Z_0^m$ labeled \"reduction\" on the left.\n- Vertical downward arrow from $B/K, K/K_0 \\text{ finite}$ to $B_0$ labeled \"reduction\" on the right.\n- Horizontal rightward arrow from $Z_0^m$ to $B_0$ labeled \"$a_{\\mathrm{fl}}$\".\n- Centered formula below the lower boxes: \"$a_{\\mathrm{fl}}^* \\operatorname{Fil}^1 H_{\\mathrm{dR}}^1(B) = (F_Z^1)^{\\oplus m}$\"."
